\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{TV}(p_{X_{T}},p_{Y_{t}^{\star}})+\sum\limits_{{t}={2}}^{T-1}\left(\frac{8\overline{\alpha}_{t}\eta_{t}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{3}}+C(\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}-\widetilde{\sigma}_{t+1}^{2})\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right)\right]

(107)

\displaystyle+\left(C\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}+\frac{8\overline{\alpha}_{t}\eta_{t}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{3}}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid T}(X_{T})\right)\right]+\frac{C}{T^{9}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{\leq}\mathsf{TV}(p_{X_{T}},p_{\overline{Y}_{T}}){+}\left(\frac{C\log T}{T}\right)^{2}\sum\limits_{{t}={1}}^{T-1}\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{C\log T}{T}\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{T}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid T}(X_{T})\right)\right]+\frac{1}{T^{9}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(b)}}{\leq}\mathsf{TV}(p_{X_{T}},p_{\overline{Y}_{T}})+C_{3}kT\log T\left(\frac{C\log T}{T}\right)^{2}+\frac{C_{3}Ck\log^{2}T}{T}+\frac{C}{T^{9}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T^{10}}+C^{2}C_{3}\frac{k\log^{3}T}{T}+\frac{C}{T^{9}}\leq C_{8}\frac{k\log^{3}T}{T}.

Here, (a) applies Lemma
4
and the basic property (
45
),
(b) makes use of the moment inequality (
44
) with
l=2
, whereas the penultimate inequality results from
Li and Yan (2024a, Lemma 10)
.

It remains to bound the TV distance between
p_{Y_{1}}
and
p_{\overline{Y}_{1}}
. Towards this, observe that

\displaystyle\mathsf{TV}(p_{Y_{1}},p_{\overline{Y}_{1}})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(p_{\overline{Y}_{1}}(x)-p_{{Y}_{1}}(x)\right)\mathbbm{1}\{p_{\overline{Y}_{1}}(x)>p_{Y_{1}}(x)\}{\textnormal{d}}x+\mathbb{P}(\overline{Y}_{1}=\infty)

(108)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{\leq}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(p_{\overline{Y}_{1}}(x)-p_{\widehat{Y}_{1}}(x)\right)\mathbbm{1}\{p_{\overline{Y}_{1}}(x)>p_{\widehat{Y}_{1}}(x)\}{\textnormal{d}}x+\mathbb{P}(\overline{Y}_{1}=\infty)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(b)}}{\leq}\mathsf{TV}(p_{\overline{Y}_{1}},p_{\widehat{Y}_{1}})+\mathsf{TV}(p_{X_{1}},p_{\overline{Y}_{1}})\overset{\text{(c)}}{\leq}\sqrt{\mathsf{KL}(p_{\overline{Y}_{1}}\parallel p_{\widehat{Y}_{1}})}+C\frac{k\log^{3}T}{T},

where (a) holds due to Lemma
13
, (b) follows since
{\mathbb{P}}(\overline{Y}_{1}=\infty)\leq\mathsf{TV}(X_{1},\overline{Y}_{1})
, and (c) invokes Pinsker’s inequality and (
107
).
Combine (
106
), (
107
) and (
108
) to reach

\displaystyle\mathsf{TV}(p_{X_{1}},p_{Y_{1}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{TV}(p_{X_{1}},p_{\overline{Y}_{1}})+\mathsf{TV}(p_{\overline{Y}_{1}},p_{Y_{1}})

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{k\log^{3}T}{T}+C\frac{k\log^{3}T}{T}+\sqrt{\mathsf{KL}(p_{\overline{Y}_{1}}\parallel p_{\widehat{Y}_{1}})}

\displaystyle\leq 2C\frac{k\log^{3}T}{T}+\sqrt{c_{1}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score}}^{2}\log T}=C\frac{k\log^{3}T}{T}+\sqrt{c_{1}C\log T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score}},

thereby concluding the proof of Theorem
5
.

D.3
Proof of Lemma
13

We can invoke induction to establish this result. To begin with, it can be easily checked that
p_{Y_{T}}=p_{\widehat{Y}_{T}}
. Next, suppose that the claim (
89
) holds for
t+1
, then it follows that

\displaystyle p_{\widehat{Y}_{t}}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}p_{\widehat{Y}_{t}\,|\,\widehat{Y}_{t}^{-}}(x\,|\,x^{\prime})p_{\widehat{Y}_{t}^{-}}(x^{\prime}){\textnormal{d}}x^{\prime}=\left(\frac{p_{X_{t}}(x)}{p_{\overline{Y}_{t}^{-}}(x)}\wedge 1\right)p_{\widehat{Y}_{t}^{-}}(x)\leq p_{\widehat{Y}_{t}^{-}}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}p_{\widehat{Y}_{t}^{-}\,|\,\widehat{Y}_{t+1}}(x\,|\,x^{\prime})p_{\widehat{Y}_{t+1}}(x^{\prime}){\textnormal{d}}x^{\prime}\leq\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}p_{Y_{t}\,|\,Y_{t+1}}(x\,|\,x^{\prime})p_{Y_{t+1}}(x^{\prime}){\textnormal{d}}x^{\prime}=p_{Y_{t}}(x),

thus validating the claim (
89
) for
t
. This immediately concludes the proof by induction.

D.4
Proof of Lemma
14

We still use Lemma
12
to prove this lemma.
In fact, what we need to do is verify that
\det\left(\frac{\partial u_{t}(x)}{\partial x}\right)\neq 0,~\forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
\lim\limits_{x\to\infty}\left\|{u_{t}(x)}\right\|_{2}=\infty
.
On the one hand, owing to Tweedie’s formula (
46
), it can be derived that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{t}(x)}{\partial x}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial(x+\eta_{t}s_{t}^{\star}(x))}{\partial x}=I+\eta_{t}\left(\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)-\frac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}I\right)

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)I+\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}\eta_{t}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)\succeq\left(1-\frac{\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)I\succeq\frac{1}{2}I.

Here, the last inequality is a consequence of the step size condition
\eta_{t}\leq\frac{1}{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})
. According to above derivation, we know that
\det\left(\frac{\partial u_{t}(x)}{\partial x}\right)\neq 0
for any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.

On the other hand, Assumption
1
says that
\sup\limits_{x\in{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}}}\left\|{x}\right\|_{2}\leq T^{c_{R}}
. Hence,
\mu_{0\mid t}(x)
defined by (
47
) satisfies that

\left\|{\mu_{0\mid t}(x)}\right\|_{2}=\left\|{{\mathbb{E}}[X_{0}\mid X_{t}=x]}\right\|_{2}\leq{\mathbb{E}}[\left\|{X_{0}}\right\|_{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\leq T^{c_{R}},~\forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}.

Combining this with Tweedie’s formula (
46
) yields,

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{x\to\infty}\left\|{u_{t}(x)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{x\to\infty}\left\|{x+\eta_{t}s_{t}^{\star}(x)}\right\|_{2}=\lim\limits_{x\to\infty}\left\|{\left(1-\frac{\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)x+\frac{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0\mid t}(x)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)\lim\limits_{x\to\infty}\left\|{x}\right\|_{2}-\frac{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}T^{c_{R}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\lim\limits_{x\to\infty}\left\|{x}\right\|_{2}-\frac{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}T^{c_{R}}=\infty.

This completes the proof of the lemma.

D.5
Proof of Lemma
15

Suppose that conditional on
X_{0}=x_{0}
, one has
U_{t}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(\lambda_{t}x_{0},\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}I)
for some quantities
\lambda_{t}
and
\overline{\sigma}_{t}>0
.
Denoting by
\widetilde{p}_{U_{t}|X_{0}}
the conditional density of
U_{t}
given
X_{0}
, one can easily see that: the distribution associated with the pdf
\widetilde{q}_{t}(x_{t-1})=\int_{x_{t}}p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}^{\star}}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})\widetilde{p}_{U_{t}|X_{0}}(u_{t}|x_{0}){\textnormal{d}}u_{t}
is

{\mathcal{N}}\left(\frac{\lambda_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}x_{0},~\frac{\sigma_{t}^{2}+\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}{\alpha_{t}}\right).

By taking

\lambda_{t}=\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\cdot\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}=\alpha_{t}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})-{\sigma_{t}^{2}},

we see that the above Gaussian distribution coincides with
{\mathcal{N}}\left(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0},1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}\right)
, which is precisely the distribution of
X_{t-1}
given
X_{0}=x_{0}
.

D.6
Proof of Lemma
16

By combining Lemma
14
and the basic change of variable formula, we can do the following computation,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{t-1}(x_{t-1})=p_{X_{t-1}}(x_{t-1})-\int p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}^{\star}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(x_{t}\,|\,x_{0})p_{X_{0}}(x_{0}){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}x_{t}

\displaystyle=p_{X_{t-1}}(x_{t-1})-\int p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}^{\star}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})\widetilde{p}_{U_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}\big(u_{t}(x_{t})\,|\,x_{0}\big){\frac{p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}\big(x_{t}\,|\,x_{0}\big)}{\widetilde{p}_{U_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}\big(u_{t}(x_{t})\,|\,x_{0}\big)}}p_{X_{0}}(x_{0}){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}x_{t}

\displaystyle=p_{X_{t-1}}(x_{t-1})-\int p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}^{\star}}\big(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t}(u_{t})\big)\widetilde{p}_{U_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(u_{t}\,|\,x_{0}){\frac{p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}\big(x_{t}(u_{t})\,|\,x_{0}\big)}{\widetilde{p}_{U_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(u_{t}\,|\,x_{0})}}\det\left(\frac{{\textnormal{d}}x_{t}}{{\textnormal{d}}u_{t}}\right)p_{X_{0}}(x_{0}){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}u_{t}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\left(\int p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{0}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{0})p_{X_{0}}(x_{0}){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}-\int p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}^{\star}}\big(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t}(u_{t})\big)\widetilde{p}_{U_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(u_{t}\,|\,x_{0})p_{X_{0}}(x_{0}){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}u_{t}\right)}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{R}}_{t-1}^{\prime}(x_{t-1})}

\displaystyle-\int p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}^{\star}}\big(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t}(u_{t})\big)\widetilde{p}_{U_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(u_{t}\,|\,x_{0}){\left(\frac{p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(x_{t}(u_{t})\,|\,x_{0})}{\widetilde{p}_{U_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(u_{t}\,|\,x_{0})}\det\left(\frac{{\textnormal{d}}x_{t}}{{\textnormal{d}}u_{t}}\right)-1\right)}p_{X_{0}}(x_{0}){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}u_{t}.

(109)

Regarding the first term in (
109
), it is readily seen from (
97
) that

{\mathcal{R}}_{t-1}^{\prime}(x_{t-1})=0.

When it comes to the second term in (
109
), it follows from the definition of
u_{t}
(cf. (
96
)) that

\displaystyle-\int p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}^{\star}}\big(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t}(u_{t})\big)\widetilde{p}_{U_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(u_{t}\,|\,x_{0}){\left(\frac{p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}\big(x_{t}(u_{t})\,|\,x_{0}\big)}{\widetilde{p}_{U_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(u_{t}\,|\,x_{0})}\det\left(\frac{{\textnormal{d}}x_{t}}{{\textnormal{d}}u_{t}}\right)-1\right)}p_{X_{0}}(x_{0}){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}u_{t}

\displaystyle=-\int p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}^{\star}}\big(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t}(u_{t})\big)p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}\big(x_{t}(u_{t})\,|\,x_{0}\big)\det\left(\frac{{\textnormal{d}}x_{t}}{{\textnormal{d}}u_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left(1-\frac{\widetilde{p}_{U_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(u_{t}\,|\,x_{0})}{p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}\big(x_{t}(u_{t})\,|\,x_{0}\big)}\det\left(\frac{{\textnormal{d}}u_{t}}{{\textnormal{d}}x_{t}}\right)\right)p_{X_{0}}(x_{0}){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}u_{t}

\displaystyle=\int p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}^{\star}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(x_{t}\,|\,x_{0})\left(\frac{\widetilde{p}_{U_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}\big(u_{t}(x_{t})\,|\,x_{0}\big)}{p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(x_{t}\,|\,x_{0})}\det\left(\frac{{\textnormal{d}}u_{t}}{{\textnormal{d}}x_{t}}\right)-1\right)p_{X_{0}}(x_{0}){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}x_{t}

\displaystyle=\int p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\mid Y_{t}^{\star}}(x_{t-1}\mid x_{t})p_{X_{t}\mid X_{0}}(x_{t}\mid x_{0})\big({\mathcal{G}}(x_{t},x_{0})-1\big)p_{X_{0}}(x_{0}){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}x_{t}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.

D.7
Proof of Lemma
17

From Tweedie’s formula (
46
), we can further simplify
{\mathcal{G}}_{1}(x_{t},x_{0})
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{1}(x_{t},x_{0})

\displaystyle=\det\left(I+\eta_{t}\frac{\partial s_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})}{\partial x_{t}}\right)\cdot\frac{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{d/2}}{\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{d}}

(110)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{=}\det\left(I+\eta_{t}\left\{\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x_{t})-\frac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}I\right\}\right)\cdot\frac{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{d/2}}{\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{d}}

\displaystyle=\det\left(\left(1-\frac{\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)I+\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}\eta_{t}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x_{t})\right)\cdot\frac{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{d/2}}{\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{d}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(b)}}{=}\det\left(I+\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}\eta_{t}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}-\eta_{t})}\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x_{t})\right)=\det\left(I+\overline{\alpha}_{t}\eta^{(1)}_{t}\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x_{t})\right),

where we define
\eta^{(1)}_{t}\coloneqq\frac{\eta_{t}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}-\eta_{t})}
. Here, (a) follows from (
46
), whereas (b) can be shown by combining
\det(\lambda A)=\lambda^{d}\det(A)
and the relation (
26
).

We then move on to
{\mathcal{G}}_{2}(x_{t},x_{0})
. Towards this end, we make the observation that

\displaystyle\log{\mathcal{G}}_{2}(x_{t},x_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{\left\|{x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}-\frac{\left\|{u_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{2\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}=\frac{\left\|{x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}-\frac{\left\|{x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}+\eta_{t}s_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{2\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}

(111)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{-}\frac{1}{2\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\right)\left\|{x_{t}{-}\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}}\right\|_{2}^{2}{-}\frac{\eta_{t}}{\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}(x_{t}{-}\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0})^{\top}s_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}){-}\frac{\eta_{t}^{2}}{2\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\left\|{s_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{=}-\frac{\eta_{t}}{\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}(x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0})^{\top}\big(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0|t}(x_{t})-x_{t}\big)-\frac{\eta_{t}^{2}}{2\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\left\|{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0|t}(x_{t})-x_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{-}\frac{1}{2\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\right)\left\|{x_{t}{-}\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{-}\frac{1}{2\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\right)\left\|{x_{t}{-}\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left(\frac{\eta_{t}}{\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}-\frac{\eta_{t}^{2}}{2\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\right)\left\|{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0|t}(x_{t})-x_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\eta_{t}}{\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\big(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0|t}(x_{t})\big)^{\top}\big(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0|t}(x_{t})-x_{t}\big),

where (a) follows since
s_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})=\frac{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0|t}(x_{t})-\frac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{t}
(see (
46
)). Further, in view of (
26
), one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}-\frac{1}{2\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}-(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}=\frac{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\left(1-\frac{\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{2}-(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\eta_{t}-\eta_{t}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}=-\left(\frac{\eta_{t}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}-\frac{\eta_{t}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\right).

Let us denote
\eta^{(2)}_{t}\coloneqq\frac{\eta_{t}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}-\frac{\eta_{t}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}
.
Substituting these equations into (
111
) yields

\displaystyle\log{\mathcal{G}}_{2}(x_{t},x_{0})

\displaystyle=\eta^{(2)}_{t}\left\{\left\|{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0|t}(x_{t}){-}x_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}{-}\left\|{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}{-}x_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

(112)

\displaystyle+\frac{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\eta_{t}}{\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\big(x_{0}{-}\mu_{0|t}(x_{t})\big)^{\top}\big(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0|t}(x_{t}){-}x_{t}\big)

\displaystyle=\zeta_{t}(x_{t},x_{0})+\int_{x_{0}}\log{\mathcal{G}}_{2}(x_{t},x_{0})p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x_{t}){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}=\zeta_{t}(x_{t},x_{0}){-}\overline{\alpha}_{t}\eta^{(2)}_{t}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x_{t})\right).

Here, it can be easily verified that
\zeta_{t}(x_{t},x_{0})
satisfies
\int_{x_{0}}\zeta_{t}(x_{t},x_{0})p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x_{t}){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}=0
for all
x_{t}
.

To finish up, combining (
110
) and (
112
) concludes the proof.

D.8
Proof of Lemma
18

By setting the square matrix
\Delta
in Lemma
5
to
0
, we can show that for any positive semi-definite matrix
A\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
,

\log\det\left(I+A\right)\geq\mathrm{tr}(A)-\left\|{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(113)

Equipped with this result, we can derive the following inequality:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{X_{t}}\big[(-Z_{t}(X_{t}))_{+}\big]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{X_{t}}\left[\left(\overline{\alpha}_{t}{\eta}_{t}^{\prime}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right)-\log\det\big(I+\overline{\alpha}_{t}\eta^{(1)}_{t}\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\big)\right)_{+}\right]

(114)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}{\mathbb{E}}_{X_{t}}\left[\left(\overline{\alpha}_{t}\left(\eta^{(2)}_{t}-\eta^{(1)}_{t}\right)\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right)+{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}\big(\eta^{(1)}_{t}\big)^{2}}\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)_{+}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\overline{\alpha}_{t}\left(\eta^{(2)}_{t}-\eta^{(1)}_{t}\right)_{+}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right)\right]+{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}\big(\eta^{(1)}_{t}\big)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right].

Here, (a) holds by combining (
113
) and the fact that the function
(\cdot)_{+}
is non-decreasing and the last inequality follows from
(a+b)_{+}\leq(a)_{+}+(b)_{+}
.

In order to further bound (
114
), let us inspect the coefficients
\overline{\alpha}_{t}\big(\eta^{(2)}_{t}-\eta^{(1)}_{t}\big)_{+}
and
{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}\big(\eta^{(1)}_{t}\big)^{2}}
. Combining the definitions of
\eta^{(1)}_{t},~\eta^{(2)}_{t}
and the relation (
26
) results in

\displaystyle\overline{\alpha}_{t}\left(\eta^{(2)}_{t}-\eta^{(1)}_{t}\right)_{+}

\displaystyle=\overline{\alpha}_{t}\left(\frac{\eta_{t}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}-\frac{\eta_{t}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}-\frac{\eta_{t}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}-\eta_{t})}\right)_{+}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\cdot\frac{\left(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}-\eta_{t}-\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}\right)_{+}}{\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}-\eta_{t})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\cdot\frac{\left(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}-\eta_{t}-(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\left(1-\frac{2\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}+\frac{\eta_{t}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\right)\right)_{+}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}-\eta_{t})\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\cdot\frac{\left(\eta_{t}-\eta_{t}^{2}\big/(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\right)_{+}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}-\eta_{t})\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\leq\frac{8\overline{\alpha}_{t}\eta_{t}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{3}},

where the last inequality holds due to
\eta_{t}\leq\frac{1}{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})
and
\overline{\sigma}_{t}^{2}=(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\left(1-\frac{\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{2}\geq(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}=4(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})
. Applying similar arguments, we can also derive

\displaystyle{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}\big(\eta^{(1)}_{t}\big)^{2}}=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}-\eta_{t})^{2}}\leq\frac{4\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\leq\frac{4C^{2}(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}=4C^{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{4},

where
\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}(1-\alpha_{t})}{(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}
.

Additionally, Lemma
3
tells us that

\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{4}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]\leq 3\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}\left\{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\big(\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\big)\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\big(\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid{t-1}}(X_{t-1})\big)\right]\right\}+\frac{3}{T^{10}}.

Plugging the above results into (
114
), we complete the proof.

Appendix E
Equivalence between relation (
26
) and
Song et al. (2020, Eq. (12))

Recall that

\epsilon_{t}^{\mathsf{noise}}(Y_{t})=-\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}s_{t}(Y_{t}).

Substituting this expression into (
28
) (i.e.,
Song et al. (2020, Eq. (12))
) results in:

\displaystyle Y_{t-1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\Big(Y_{t}+(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})s_{t}(Y_{t})-\sqrt{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\varsigma_{t}^{2})}\,s_{t}(Y_{t})+\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\varsigma_{t}Z_{t}\Big).

By taking

\eta_{t}^{\mathsf{ddpm}}=(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})-\sqrt{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\varsigma_{t}^{2})}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sigma_{t}^{\mathsf{ddpm}}=\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\varsigma_{t},

we can derive

\displaystyle(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\left(1-\frac{\eta_{t}^{\mathsf{ddpm}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\Bigg(1-\frac{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})-\sqrt{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\big(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}-\big(\sigma_{t}^{\mathsf{ddpm}}\big)^{2}\big)}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle=(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\left(\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}-\big(\sigma_{t}^{\mathsf{ddpm}}\big)^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\right)^{2}=\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}-\big(\sigma_{t}^{\mathsf{ddpm}}\big)^{2},

which is precisely the relation in (
26
).

Appendix F
Proofs about reverse-time differential equations

F.1
Generalized reverse-time differential equations

We formally state the time-reversal property of the generalized reverse-time differential equation introduced in Section
2
.

Proposition 3

Suppose the generalized reverse-time differential equation

\displaystyle{\textnormal{d}}Y_{t}=\left(Y_{t}+\big(1+\xi(T-t)\big)s^{*}_{T-t}(Y_{t})\right)\beta(T-t){\textnormal{d}}t+\sqrt{2\xi(T-t)\beta(T-t)}\,{\textnormal{d}}W_{t},\qquad t\in[0,T]

(115)

has a unique strong solution, where
(W_{t})
represents a standard Brownian motion in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Then under the boundary condition
Y_{0}\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}X_{T}
, it satisfies
Y_{T-t}\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}X_{t}
for all
0\leq t\leq T
.

Proof of Proposition
3
.

Recall that the continuous-time forward process is given by

{\textnormal{d}}X_{t}=-\beta(t)X_{t}{\textnormal{d}}t+\sqrt{2\beta(t)}\,{\textnormal{d}}B_{t},

with
(B_{t})
a standard Brownian motion in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Denote by
p_{X}(x,t)
the probability density of
X_{t}
at point
x
w.r.t. the Lebesgue measure in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
In the following proof, we use
\nabla
(resp.
\nabla\cdot
) to be the gradient (resp. divergence) operator taken w.r.t. the first argument (i.e.,
x
) of the function, and denote by
\Delta
the corresponding Laplace operator.
The Fokker-Planck equation then tells us that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p_{X}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\nabla\cdot\big(x\beta(t)p_{X}(x,t)\big)+\frac{1}{2}\Delta\big(2\beta(t)p_{X}(x,t)\big)

\displaystyle=\beta(t)\nabla\cdot\big(xp_{X}(x,t)\big)+\beta(t)\Delta\big(p_{X}(x,t)\big).

(116)

Similarly, denoting by
p_{Y}(x,t)
the probability density of
Y_{t}
at point
x
w.r.t. the Lebesgue measure in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, then we can
apply the Fokker-Planck equation once again to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p_{Y}(x,t)

\displaystyle=-\nabla\cdot\bigg(\Big(x+\big(1+\xi(T-t)\big)s^{*}_{T-t}(x)\Big)\beta(T-t)p_{Y}(x,t)\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\Delta\big(2\xi(T-t)\beta(T-t)p_{Y}(x,t)\big)

\displaystyle=-\Big\langle x+\big(1+\xi(T-t)\big)s^{*}_{T-t}(x),\,\beta(T-t)\nabla p_{Y}(x,t)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\mathrm{tr}\left(I_{d}+\big(1+\xi(T-t)\big)\nabla s^{*}_{T-t}(x)\right)\beta(T-t)p_{Y}(x,t)

\displaystyle\qquad+\xi(T-t)\beta(T-t)\Delta\big(p_{Y}(x,t)\big).

(117)

Recall that our goal is to show that
X_{t}
and
Y_{T-t}
have the same marginal distributions, i.e.,
p_{X}(x,t)=p_{Y}(x,T-t)
, or equivalently,
p_{X}(x,T-t)=p_{Y}(x,t)
.
Since the generalized differential equation (
115
) is assumed to have a unique strong solution, the induced Fokker-Planck equation has a unique strong solution. From the assumption
Y_{0}\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}X_{T}
, we know that
p_{X}(x,T)=p_{Y}(x,0)
, and hence it suffices to show that
p_{X}(x,T-t)
is a solution of the partial differential equation (PDE) (
117
).
It is readily seen from PDE (
116
) that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p_{X}(x,T-t)=-\beta(T-t)\nabla\cdot\big(xp_{X}(x,T-t)\big)-\beta(T-t)\Delta\big(p_{X}(x,T-t)\big).

(118)

Replacing
p_{Y}(x,t)
with
p_{X}(x,T-t)
on the right-hand side of PDE (
117
), one can derive

\displaystyle-\Big\langle x+\big(1+\xi(T-t)\big)s^{*}_{T-t}(x),\,\beta(T-t)\nabla p_{X}(x,T-t)\Big\rangle-\mathrm{tr}(I_{d})\beta(T-t)p_{X}(x,T-t)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt-\big(1+\xi(T-t)\big)\beta(T-t)\Delta\big(\log(p_{X}(x,T-t))\big)p_{X}(x,T-t)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+\xi(T-t)\beta(T-t)\Delta\big(p_{X}(x,T-t)\big)

\displaystyle=-\beta(T-t)\bigg\langle x+\big(1+\xi(T-t)\big)\frac{\nabla p_{X}(x,T-t)}{p_{X}(x,T-t)},\nabla p_{X}(x,T-t)\bigg\rangle-d\beta(T-t)p_{X}(x,T-t)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\big(1+\xi(T-t)\big)\beta(T-t)\frac{\|\nabla p_{X}(x,T-t)\|_{2}^{2}}{p_{X}(x,T-t)}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\Big(-\big(1+\xi(T-t)\big)+\xi(T-t)\Big)\beta(T-t)\Delta\big(p_{X}(x,T-t)\big)

\displaystyle=-\beta(T-t)\big\langle x,\nabla p_{X}(x,T-t)\big\rangle-d\beta(T-t)p_{X}(x,T-t)-\beta(T-t)\Delta\big(p_{X}(x,T-t)\big)

\displaystyle=-\beta(T-t)\nabla\cdot\big(xp_{X}(x,T-t)\big)-\beta(T-t)\Delta\big(p_{X}(x,T-t)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p_{X}(x,T-t),

(119)

where we invoke PDE (
118
) in the last line. Eqn. (
119
) reveals that
p_{X}(x,T-t)
is a strong solution of PDE (
117
), which is equivalent to
p_{X}(x,T-t)=p_{Y}(x,t)
.

F.2
Proof of Proposition
2

We prove this result by explicitly solving SDE (
35
) when
t\in[t_{n},t_{n+1})
.
To begin with, we make the observation that: under the time transformation

t~\rightarrow~t^{\prime}=\int_{0}^{t}\beta(s){\textnormal{d}}s,

(120)

SDE (
35
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle{\textnormal{d}}\widetilde{Y}_{t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\left(-\frac{\xi(T-t_{n}^{\prime})+\overline{\alpha}_{T-t^{\prime}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{T-t^{\prime}}}\widetilde{Y}_{t^{\prime}}+\frac{\left(1+\xi(T-t_{n}^{\prime})\right)\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T-t^{\prime}}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{T-t^{\prime}}}\mu_{T-t^{\prime}_{n}}(\widetilde{Y}_{t^{\prime}_{n}})\right){\textnormal{d}}t^{\prime}+\sqrt{2\xi(T-t_{n}^{\prime})}\,{\textnormal{d}}W_{t^{\prime}}

for
t\in[t_{n}^{\prime},t_{n+1}^{\prime})
, where
t_{n}^{\prime}
and
t_{n+1}^{\prime}
are the images of
t_{n}
and
t_{n+1}
under the transformation (
120
). Note that this transformed SDE has the same form as SDE (
35
) when
\beta(t)=1
for
t\in[t_{n}^{\prime},t_{n+1}^{\prime})
.
Thus, without loss of generality, it suffices to assume
\beta(t)=1
for all
t\in[0,T]
and solve SDE (
35
). Under this assumption, we can simplify

\displaystyle\overline{\alpha}_{t}=\exp\left(-2\int_{0}^{t}\beta(s){\textnormal{d}}s\right)=e^{-2t}.

(121)

Recall that SDE (
35
) with
\xi(T-t_{n})=\xi>0
and
\beta(t)=1
can be written as

\displaystyle{\textnormal{d}}\widetilde{Y}_{t}=\left(-\frac{\xi+\overline{\alpha}_{T-t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{T-t}}\widetilde{Y}_{t}+\frac{\left(1+\xi\right)\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T-t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{T-t}}\mu_{T-t_{n}}(\widetilde{Y}_{t_{n}})\right){\textnormal{d}}t+\sqrt{2\xi}\,{\textnormal{d}}W_{t}.

(122)

To solve SDE (
122
), we find it convenient to introduce the following function

\displaystyle f(t)=\frac{e^{-\xi(T-t)}}{(1-e^{-2(T-t)})^{\frac{1+\xi}{2}}}.

It follows from Itô’s formula that

\displaystyle{\textnormal{d}}\left(f(t)\widetilde{Y}_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\left(f^{\prime}(t)-\frac{\xi+\overline{\alpha}_{T-t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{T-t}}f(t)\right)\widetilde{Y}_{t}+\frac{\left(1+\xi\right)\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T-t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{T-t}}f(t)\mu_{T-t_{n}}(\widetilde{Y}_{t_{n}})\Bigg){\textnormal{d}}t+\sqrt{2\xi}f(t)\,{\textnormal{d}}W_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(1+\xi\right)\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T-t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{T-t}}f(t)\mu_{T-t_{n}}(\widetilde{Y}_{t_{n}}){\textnormal{d}}t+\sqrt{2\xi}f(t)\,{\textnormal{d}}W_{t}.

Integrating both sides of the above display from
t_{n}
to
t_{n+1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle f(t_{n+1})\widetilde{Y}_{t_{n+1}}-f(t_{n})\widetilde{Y}_{t_{n}}=\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\frac{\left(1+\xi\right)\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T-t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{T-t}}f(t)\mu_{T-t_{n}}(\widetilde{Y}_{t_{n}}){\textnormal{d}}t+\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\sqrt{2\xi}f(t)\,{\textnormal{d}}W_{t}.

From Itô’s isometry property of the Brownian motion, we can write, for each
0\leq n\leq T-1
,

\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\sqrt{2\xi}f(t)\,{\textnormal{d}}W_{t}=\left(\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}2\xi\big(f(t)\big)^{2}{\textnormal{d}}t\right)^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{n}

for some Gaussian vector
\widetilde{Z}_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
,
where
\{\widetilde{Z}_{n}\}_{n=0,\dots,T-1}
are statistically independent.
Consequently,

\displaystyle f(t_{n+1})\widetilde{Y}_{t_{n+1}}=f(t_{n})\widetilde{Y}_{t_{n}}+\underbrace{\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\frac{\left(1+\xi\right)\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T-t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{T-t}}f(t){\textnormal{d}}t}_{\eqqcolon\,A_{n}}\cdot\mu_{T-t_{n}}(\widetilde{Y}_{t_{n}})+\bigg(\underbrace{\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}2\xi\big(f(t)\big)^{2}{\textnormal{d}}t}_{\eqqcolon\,B_{n}}\bigg)^{1/2}\cdot\widetilde{Z}_{n}.

Taking this together with the definition (
33b
) of
\mu_{t}
, we can express the update rule induced by SDE (
122
) as

\displaystyle\widetilde{Y}_{t_{n+1}}=\frac{f(t_{n})+A_{n}/\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T-t_{n}}}}{f(t_{n+1})}\widetilde{Y}_{t_{n}}+\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{T-t_{n}}}{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T-t_{n}}}}\cdot\frac{A_{n}}{f(t_{n+1})}s_{T-t_{n}}(\widetilde{Y}_{t_{n}})+\frac{B_{n}}{f(t_{n+1})}\widetilde{Z}_{n}.

(123)

To simplify the notation, we define

\gamma_{n}=e^{-(T-t_{n})}.

The terms
A_{n}
and
B_{n}
can be explicitly calculated as follows:

\displaystyle A_{n}

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\frac{\left(1+\xi\right)\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T-t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{T-t}}f(t){\textnormal{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\left(1+\xi\right)\frac{e^{-(1+\xi)(T-t)}}{\left(1-e^{-2(T-t)}\right)^{(3+\xi)/2}}\,{\textnormal{d}}t

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-(1+\xi)(T-t)}}{\left(1-e^{-2(T-t)}\right)^{(1+\xi)/2}}\,\Bigg|_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}=\frac{\gamma_{n+1}^{\xi+1}}{(1-\gamma_{n+1}^{2})^{\frac{1+\xi}{2}}}-\frac{\gamma_{n}^{\xi+1}}{(1-\gamma_{n}^{2})^{\frac{1+\xi}{2}}},

(124)

where we have applied (
121
).
Through similar calculation, we can reach

\displaystyle B_{n}

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}2\xi\big(f(t)\big)^{2}{\textnormal{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}2\xi\frac{e^{-2\xi(T-t)}}{\left(1-e^{-2(T-t)}\right)^{1+\xi}}\,{\textnormal{d}}t

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-2\xi(T-t)}}{\left(1-e^{-2(T-t)}\right)^{\xi}}\,\Bigg|_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}=\frac{\gamma_{n+1}^{2\xi}}{(1-\gamma_{n+1}^{2})^{\xi}}-\frac{\gamma_{n}^{2\xi}}{(1-\gamma_{n}^{2})^{\xi}}.

(125)

Substituting (
124
) and (
125
) into (
123
) and comparing the coefficients with the DDPM update rule (
4
), we obtain

\displaystyle\alpha_{t_{n}}=\frac{f(t_{n})+A_{n}/\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T-t_{n}}}}{f(t_{n+1})}=\left(\frac{\gamma_{n}}{\gamma_{n+1}}\right)^{2}=e^{-2(t_{n+1}-t_{n})},

(126)

which coincides with our choice of
\overline{\alpha}_{t}
, i.e.,

\alpha_{t_{n}}=e^{-2(t_{n+1}-t_{n})}=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t_{n+1}}}{\overline{\alpha}_{t_{n}}}.

Additionally, we can easily verify that

\displaystyle\eta_{t_{n}}^{\mathsf{ddpm}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\alpha_{t_{n}}}\cdot\frac{1-\gamma_{n}^{2}}{\gamma_{n}}\cdot\frac{A_{n}}{f(t_{n+1})}=\frac{1-\gamma_{n}^{2}}{\gamma_{n+1}}\cdot\frac{1}{f(t_{n+1})}\cdot\left(\frac{\gamma_{n+1}^{\xi+1}}{(1-\gamma_{n+1}^{2})^{\frac{1+\xi}{2}}}-\frac{\gamma_{n}^{\xi+1}}{(1-\gamma_{n}^{2})^{\frac{1+\xi}{2}}}\right),

\displaystyle\sigma_{t_{n}}^{\mathsf{ddpm}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\alpha_{t_{n}}}\cdot\frac{B_{n}^{1/2}}{f(t_{n+1})}=\frac{\gamma_{n}}{\gamma_{n+1}f(t_{n+1})}\cdot\left(\frac{\gamma_{n+1}^{2\xi}}{(1-\gamma_{n+1}^{2})^{\xi}}-\frac{\gamma_{n}^{2\xi}}{(1-\gamma_{n}^{2})^{\xi}}\right)^{1/2}.

We are now ready to show that the relation (
26
)

(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t_{n}})\bigg(1-\frac{\eta_{t_{n}}^{\mathsf{ddpm}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t_{n}}}\bigg)^{2}=\alpha_{t_{n}}-\overline{\alpha}_{t_{n}}-\big(\sigma_{t_{n}}^{\mathsf{ddpm}}\big)^{2}

is satisfied by this solution for all
n
.
Towards this end, calculate the left-hand side above as:

\displaystyle(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t_{n}})\bigg(1-\frac{\eta_{t_{n}}^{\mathsf{ddpm}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t_{n}}}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma_{n}^{2})\left(1-\frac{1}{\gamma_{n+1}f(t_{n+1})}\cdot\left(\frac{\gamma_{n+1}^{\xi+1}}{(1-\gamma_{n+1}^{2})^{\frac{1+\xi}{2}}}-\frac{\gamma_{n}^{\xi+1}}{(1-\gamma_{n}^{2})^{\frac{1+\xi}{2}}}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma_{n}^{2})\left(\frac{\gamma_{n}^{\xi+1}}{\gamma_{n+1}^{\xi+1}}\cdot\frac{(1-\gamma_{n+1}^{2})^{\frac{1+\xi}{2}}}{(1-\gamma_{n}^{2})^{\frac{1+\xi}{2}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{n}^{2(\xi+1)}}{\gamma_{n+1}^{2(\xi+1)}}\cdot\frac{(1-\gamma_{n+1}^{2})^{1+\xi}}{(1-\gamma_{n}^{2})^{\xi}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{n}^{2}}{\gamma_{n+1}^{2}}-\gamma_{n}^{2}-(1-\gamma_{n+1}^{2})\left(\frac{\gamma_{n}^{2}}{\gamma_{n+1}^{2}}-\frac{\gamma_{n}^{2(\xi+1)}}{\gamma_{n+1}^{2(\xi+1)}}\cdot\frac{(1-\gamma_{n+1}^{2})^{\xi}}{(1-\gamma_{n}^{2})^{\xi}}\right)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t_{n}}-\overline{\alpha}_{t_{n}}-\big(\sigma_{t_{n}}^{\mathsf{ddpm}}\big)^{2}.

Thus, setting
t_{n}
=
T-n
for
n=0,1,\ldots,T
exactly recovers the relation (
26
).

F.3
Proof of Proposition
1

Proposition
1
can be regarded as a corollary of Proposition
2
in the following sense: if we set
\xi(T-t_{n})=0
for all
n=0,1,\ldots,T-1
, then SDE (
35
) degenerates to ODE (
34
). In addition, the whole proof of Proposition
2
in Appendix
F.2
works for
\xi(T-t_{n})=0
. Thus, the proof of Proposition
1
can be directly completed by repeating the proof arguments in Appendix
F.2
.

Appendix G
Proof of the lower bound in Theorem
7

Let
X_{0}\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(0,{\footnotesize\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}I_{k}\\
&0\end{array}\right]}\right)
, then it follows from (
7
) that

\displaystyle X_{t}=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\,\overline{W}_{t}\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(0,\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}I_{k}\\
&(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})I_{d-k}\end{array}\right]\right).

It is then easily seen that

\displaystyle s_{t}^{*}(x)=-\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}I_{k}\\
&(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})I_{d-k}\end{array}\right]^{-1}x=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}I_{k}\\
&\frac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}I_{d-k}\end{array}\right]x.

As a result, the mapping
\Phi_{t}^{*}
admits a closed-form expression as follows

\Phi_{t}^{*}(x,z)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha}_{t}}\big(x+\eta_{t}s_{t}^{*}(x)+\sigma_{t}z\big)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha}_{t}}(A_{\eta_{t}}x+\sigma_{t}z)

where

\displaystyle A_{\eta_{t}}\coloneqq\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}(1-\eta_{t})I_{k}\\
&\big(1-\frac{\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\big)I_{d-k}\end{array}\right].

These properties taken together further imply that

\displaystyle\Phi_{t}^{*}(X_{t},Z_{t})\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(0,\,\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}}A_{\eta_{t}}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}I_{k}\\
&(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})I_{d-k}\end{array}\right]A_{\eta_{t}}+\frac{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\alpha_{t}}I_{d}\right),

or equivalently,

\displaystyle\Phi_{t}^{*}(X_{t},Z_{t})\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(0,\,\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{(1-\eta_{t})^{2}}{\alpha_{t}}I_{k}\\
&\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\big(1-\frac{\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\big)^{2}I_{d-k}\end{array}\right]+\frac{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\alpha_{t}}I_{d}\right).

Armed with the above basic properties,
we can proceed to derive the advertised lower bound. Towards this end, we resort to the following result concerning the TV distance between two multivariate Gaussians with the same mean, whose proof can be found in
Devroye et al. (2018)
.

Lemma 19
(TV distance between Gaussians with the same mean)

Consider any
\mu\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, and any positive semidefinite matrices
\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
.
Then it holds that

\frac{1}{100}<\frac{\mathsf{TV}\big({\mathcal{N}}(\mu,\Sigma_{1}),~{\mathcal{N}}(\mu,\Sigma_{2})\big)}{\min\left\{1,~\left\|{\Sigma_{1}^{-1}\Sigma_{2}-I}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right\}}\leq\frac{3}{2}.

Recall from (
G
) that

\displaystyle X_{t-1}\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(0,\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}I_{k}\\
&(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})I_{d-k}\end{array}\right]\right).

With this and (
G
) in mind, we take

\displaystyle\Sigma_{1}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}I_{k}\\
&(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})I_{d-k}\end{array}\right],~~\Sigma_{2}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{(1-\eta_{t})^{2}}{\alpha_{t}}I_{k}\\
&\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\big(1-\frac{\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\big)^{2}I_{d-k}\end{array}\right]+\frac{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\alpha_{t}}I_{d},

which satisfy

\Sigma_{1}^{-1}\Sigma_{2}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{(1-\eta_{t})^{2}+\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\alpha_{t}}I_{k}\\
&\left(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\left(1-\frac{\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)I_{d-k}\end{array}\right].

Invoke Lemma
19
to arrive at the following lower bound:

\displaystyle\mathsf{TV}

\displaystyle\big(X_{t-1},~\Phi_{t}^{*}(X_{t})\big)\geq\frac{1}{100}\min\left\{1,~\left\|{\Sigma_{1}^{-1}\Sigma_{2}-I}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{100}\min\left\{1,~\sqrt{k\left(\frac{(1-\eta_{t})^{2}+\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\alpha_{t}}-1\right)^{2}+(d-k)\left(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\left(1-\frac{\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}-1\right)^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{100}\min\left\{1,~\sqrt{\frac{d}{2}\left(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\left(1-\frac{\eta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}-1\right)^{2}}\right\},

where the last line follows from our assumption that
d\geq 2k
. This concludes the proof.

Appendix H
Auxiliary lemmas and related proofs

H.1
Proof of Lemma
1

Recall that
V_{\alpha}=\sqrt{\alpha}V_{1}+\sqrt{1-\alpha}Z
, where
V_{1}\sim p_{\mathsf{data}}
and
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
. From this, we can derive

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(V_{\alpha}\notin{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}\big(\sqrt{\alpha}V_{1}+\sqrt{1-\alpha}Z\notin{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}\big)\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(\bigg\{V_{1}\notin\bigcup\limits_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\bigg\}\cup\{Z\notin{\mathcal{G}}\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{j\in[N_{\epsilon_{0}}]\backslash{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathbb{P}}(V_{1}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{j})+{\mathbb{P}}(Z\notin{\mathcal{G}}).

In view of the definition (
36
) of
{\mathcal{I}}
, we know that for each
j\notin{\mathcal{I}}
,

{\mathbb{P}}(V_{1}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{j})\leq\exp\{-C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\},

with
C_{1}>0
a universal constant.
Taking this with Assumption
2
yields

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j\in[N_{\epsilon_{0}}]\backslash{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathbb{P}}(V_{1}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq N_{\epsilon_{0}}\exp\{-C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big\{C_{\mathsf{cover}}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})-C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\big\}\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{3}{8}C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\right\},

where the last inequality holds as long as
C_{1}\geq 16C_{\mathsf{cover}}
.

In addition, we can establish an upper bound on
{\mathbb{P}}(Z\notin{\mathcal{G}})
using the definition of
{\mathcal{G}}
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(Z\notin{\mathcal{G}})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\|{Z}\right\|_{2}>2\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+{\mathbb{P}}\left(\exists~1\leq i,j\leq N_{\epsilon_{0}}\text{ s.t. }\left|{(x_{i}^{*}-x_{j}^{\star})^{\top}Z}\right|>\sqrt{C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})}\left\|{x_{i}^{*}-x_{j}^{\star}}\right\|_{2}\right),

(131)

leaving us with two terms to control.

•

By virtue of the concentration property of
\chi^{2}
random variables (e.g.,
Laurent and Massart (2000, Lemma 1)
), we find that the first term on the right-hand side of (
131
) satisfies

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\|{Z}\right\|_{2}>2\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})}\right)\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{C_{1}}{2}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\right\}.

(132)

•

When it comes to the second term on the right-hand side of (
131
), we observe that: for every pair of fixed points
x_{i}^{*},x_{j}^{\star}
, one has
\frac{(x_{i}^{*}-x_{j}^{\star})^{\top}}{\|x_{i}^{*}-x_{j}^{\star}\|_{2}}Z\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
. Thus, it follows from the concentration property of standard Gaussians that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|{(x_{i}^{*}-x_{j}^{\star})^{\top}Z}\right|>\sqrt{C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})}\left\|{x_{i}^{*}-x_{j}^{\star}}\right\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|{\frac{(x_{i}^{*}-x_{j}^{\star})^{\top}}{\left\|{x_{i}^{*}-x_{j}^{\star}}\right\|_{2}}Z}\right|>\sqrt{C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{C_{1}}{2}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\right\}.

Combining this with the union-bound and Assumption
2
, we can obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}

\displaystyle\left(\exists~1\leq i,j\leq N_{\epsilon_{0}}\text{ s.t. }\left|{(x_{i}^{*}-x_{j}^{\star})^{\top}Z}\right|>\sqrt{C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})}\left\|{x_{i}^{*}-x_{j}^{\star}}\right\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{1\leq i,j\leq N_{\epsilon_{0}}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|{(x_{i}^{*}-x_{j}^{\star})^{\top}Z}\right|>\sqrt{C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})}\left\|{x_{i}^{*}-x_{j}^{\star}}\right\|_{2}\right)\leq\sum\limits_{1\leq i,j\leq N_{\epsilon_{0}}}\exp\left\{-\frac{C_{1}}{2}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq N_{\epsilon_{0}}^{2}\exp\left\{-\frac{C_{1}}{2}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\right\}\leq\exp\big\{\left(2C_{\mathsf{cover}}-C_{1}/2\right)k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\big\}\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{3}{8}C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\right\},

where the last inequality holds provided that
C_{1}\geq 16C_{\mathsf{cover}}
.
Consequently, it holds that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(Z\notin{\mathcal{G}})\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{C_{1}}{2}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\right\}+\exp\left\{-\frac{3}{8}C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\right\}\leq 2\exp\left\{-\frac{3}{8}C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\right\}.

Taking the preceding bounds together leads to

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(V_{\alpha}\notin{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha})

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{j\in[N_{\epsilon_{0}}]\backslash{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathbb{P}}(V_{1}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{j})+{\mathbb{P}}(Z\notin{\mathcal{G}})

\displaystyle\leq 3\exp\left\{-\frac{3}{8}C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\right\}\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{4}C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\right\}.

H.2
Proof of Lemma
2

Define the following set:

{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha,C}(v)\coloneqq\left\{v_{1}\,\Big|\,\sqrt{{\alpha}}\,\big\|v_{1}-x_{i(v)}^{\star}\big\|\geq\sqrt{Ck(1-{\alpha})\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})}\right\}.

Invoke the Bayes rule to obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big({\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha,C}(v)\,|\,V_{\alpha}=v\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha,C}(v)}p_{X_{0}}(v_{1})p_{V_{\alpha}\,|\,V_{1}}(v\,|\,v_{1}){\textnormal{d}}v_{1}}{\int p_{X_{0}}(\widetilde{v}_{1})p_{V_{\alpha}\,|\,V_{1}}(v|\widetilde{v}_{1}){\textnormal{d}}\widetilde{v}_{1}}\leq\frac{\int_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha,C}(v)}p_{X_{0}}(v_{1})p_{V_{\alpha}\,|\,V_{1}}(v\,|\,v_{1}){\textnormal{d}}v_{1}}{\int_{\widetilde{v}_{1}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{{i(v)}}}p_{X_{0}}(\widetilde{v}_{1})p_{V_{\alpha}\,|\,V_{1}}(v\,|\,\widetilde{v}_{1}){\textnormal{d}}\widetilde{v}_{1}}

(133)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\int_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha,C}(v)}p_{X_{0}}(v_{1})p_{V_{\alpha}\,|\,V_{1}}(v\,|\,v_{1}){\textnormal{d}}v_{1}}{{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{{i(v)}})\inf_{\widetilde{v}_{1}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{{i(v)}}}p_{V_{\alpha}\,|\,V_{1}}(v\,|\,\widetilde{v}_{1})}\leq\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{{i(v)}})}\cdot\frac{\sup_{v_{1}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha,C}(v)}p_{V_{\alpha}\,|\,V_{1}}(v\,|\,v_{1})}{\inf_{\widetilde{v}_{1}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{{i(v)}}}p_{V_{\alpha}\,|\,V_{1}}(v\,|\,\widetilde{v}_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq e^{C_{1}k\log\frac{R_{0}}{\epsilon_{0}}}\sup_{v_{1}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha,C}(v),\widetilde{v}_{1}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i(v)}}\exp\left\{\frac{1}{2(1-{\alpha})}\left[\left\|{v-\sqrt{{\alpha}}\widetilde{v}_{1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}{-}\left\|{v-\sqrt{{\alpha}}{v}_{1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\right\}.

Here, the last inequality follows from the property
{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{i(v)})\geq\exp\left(-C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\right)
, which is a direct consequence of the assumption
v\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}
and the definition (
41
) of
{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}
.

Further, consider any
(v_{1},\widetilde{v}_{1})
with
v_{1}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha,C}(v)
and
\widetilde{v}_{1}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i(v)}
. Without loss of generality, suppose
v_{1}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
. In light of the expression
v=\sqrt{{\alpha}}v_{1}^{*}+\sqrt{1-{\alpha}}\omega
, we can demonstrate that

\displaystyle\left\|{v-\sqrt{{\alpha}}\widetilde{v}_{1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|{v-\sqrt{{\alpha}}{v}_{1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=-{\alpha}\left\|{v_{1}^{*}-v_{1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\sqrt{{\alpha}(1-{\alpha})}\left\langle v_{1}-\widetilde{v}_{1},\omega\right\rangle+{\alpha}\left\|{v_{1}^{*}-\widetilde{v}_{1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{\leq}-{\alpha}\left(\big\|x_{i(v)}^{\star}-x_{j}^{\star}\big\|-2\epsilon_{0}\right)^{2}+2\sqrt{{\alpha}(1-{\alpha})}\left\langle v_{1}-\widetilde{v}_{1},\omega\right\rangle+4{\alpha}\epsilon_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(b)}}{\leq}4{\alpha}\epsilon_{0}\big\|x_{i(v)}^{\star}-x_{j}^{\star}\big\|{-}{\alpha}\big\|x_{i(v)}^{\star}{-}x_{j}^{\star}\big\|^{2}+2\sqrt{{\alpha}(1-{\alpha})}\left\{\big\langle x_{j}^{\star}-x_{i(v)}^{\star},\omega\big\rangle+2(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})})\epsilon_{0}\right\}.

Here, (a) follows from the property of the constructed
\epsilon_{0}
-net, while (b) combines the definition of the
\epsilon_{0}
-net with the norm bound for
\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}
(cf. (
37
)). Moreover, since
\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}
, it is clearly seen from (
37
) that

\big\langle x_{j}^{\star}-x_{i(v)}^{\star},\omega\big\rangle\leq\sqrt{C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})}\,\big\|x_{j}^{\star}-x_{i(v)}^{\star}\big\|.

In addition, with the choice of
\epsilon_{0}
satisfying
\epsilon_{0}\ll\sqrt{\frac{1-{\alpha}}{{\alpha}}}\min\left\{1,\sqrt{\frac{k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})}{d}}\right\}\leq\frac{1\wedge\sqrt{\frac{k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})}{d}}}{T}
, the following property holds:

4\sqrt{{\alpha}(1-{\alpha})}(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})})\epsilon_{0}\leq 5(1-{\alpha}){k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})}.

With the preceding bounds in place, we can readily obtain

\displaystyle\left\|{v-\sqrt{{\alpha}}\widetilde{v}_{1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|{v-\sqrt{{\alpha}}{v}_{1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-{\alpha}\big\|x_{i(v)}^{\star}-x_{j}^{\star}\big\|^{2}+4(1-{\alpha}){k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})}

\displaystyle+\left(2\sqrt{C_{1}{\alpha}(1-{\alpha})k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})}+4{\alpha}\epsilon_{0}\right)\big\|x_{j}^{\star}-x_{i(v)}^{\star}\big\|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{\leq}-\frac{{\alpha}}{2}\big\|x_{i(v)}^{\star}-x_{j}^{\star}\big\|^{2}+4(1-{\alpha})k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{{\alpha}}{4}\big\|x_{i(v)}^{\star}-v_{1}\big\|^{2}+4(1-{\alpha})k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{C}{4}(1-{\alpha})k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})+4(1-{\alpha})k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{C}{5}(1-{\alpha})k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0}).

Here, both (a) and the last inequality follow since
v_{1}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha,C}(v)
and
C\geq C_{2}
. Taking the above bound collectively with (
133
) yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha,C}(v)\,|\,V_{\alpha}=v)

\displaystyle\leq\exp(C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0}))

\displaystyle\times\sup_{x\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha,C}(v),\widetilde{v}_{1}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i(v)}}\exp\left\{\frac{1}{2(1-{\alpha})}\left[\left\|{v-\sqrt{{\alpha}}\widetilde{v}_{1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|{v-\sqrt{{\alpha}}{v}_{1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp(C_{1}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0}))\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{C}{5}\frac{1}{2(1-{\alpha})}(1-{\alpha})k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{C}{20}k\log(R_{0}/\epsilon_{0})\right)

as claimed.

H.3
Proof of Lemma
3

To begin with, we make note of the following result, originally developed in the stochastic localization literature
(
Eldan, 2020
)
(see also
Benton et al. (2024, Lemma 1)
),
which plays an important role in bounding the term
{\mathbb{E}}_{X_{t}}\big[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{X_{0}|X_{t}}(X_{t})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big]
.

Lemma 20

Let
\lambda_{t}\coloneqq\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}
, then for all
t>0
,

\frac{\lambda_{t}^{3}}{2\dot{\lambda}_{t}}\frac{{\textnormal{d}}}{{\textnormal{d}}t}{\mathbb{E}}_{U_{t}}\left[\mathrm{Cov}_{U_{0}|U_{t}}(U_{t})\right]={\mathbb{E}}_{U_{t}}\left[\big(\mathrm{Cov}_{U_{0}|U_{t}}(U_{t})\big)^{2}\right].

where
U_{t}\coloneqq e^{-t}X_{0}+\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}Z
with
X_{0}\sim p_{\mathsf{data}}
and
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
.
Here, we let

\mathrm{Cov}_{U_{1}|U_{t}}(u)={\mathbb{E}}[U_{1}U_{1}^{\top}\,|\,U_{t}=u]-\mathbb{E}[U_{1}\,|\,U_{t}=u]\,\mathbb{E}[U_{1}\,|\,U_{t}=u]^{\top}
,
and denote by
\dot{\lambda}_{t}
the derivative of
\lambda_{t}
with respect to
t
.

Now, let us introduce the bijection
\alpha(t)\coloneqq e^{-2t}
that maps
t\in[0,\infty)
to
\alpha\in(0,1]
. Take

\displaystyle V_{\alpha}\coloneqq\sqrt{\alpha}X_{0}+\sqrt{1-\alpha}Z,\qquad\nu_{\alpha}\coloneqq\sqrt{1-\alpha},\qquad\text{and}\qquad t(\alpha)\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{1}{\alpha}.

(134)

Then it can be readily seen that

\displaystyle V_{\alpha}=U_{t(\alpha)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\nu_{\alpha}=\lambda_{t(\alpha)}.

(135)

Straightforward calculations allow one to rewrite the result in Lemma
20
as

\displaystyle{\textnormal{d}}{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{\alpha}}\left[\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\lambda_{t(\alpha)}^{3}}\left.\frac{{\textnormal{d}}\lambda_{t}}{{\textnormal{d}}t}\right|_{t=t(\alpha)}{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{\alpha}}\left[\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}^{2}(V_{\alpha})\right]{\textnormal{d}}t(\alpha)=\frac{2\frac{{\textnormal{d}}\nu_{\alpha}}{{\textnormal{d}}\alpha}}{\nu_{\alpha}^{3}}{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{\alpha}}\left[\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}^{2}(V_{\alpha})\right]{\textnormal{d}}\alpha

(136)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(1-\alpha)^{-1/2}}{(1-\alpha)^{3/2}}{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{\alpha}}\left[\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}^{2}(V_{\alpha})\right]{\textnormal{d}}\alpha=-\frac{1}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{\alpha}}\left[\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}^{2}(V_{\alpha})\right]{\textnormal{d}}\alpha.

Integrating the above equation over the interval
[\overline{\alpha}_{t+1},\overline{\alpha}_{t})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}}^{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\frac{1}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{\alpha}}\left[\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}^{2}(V_{\alpha})\right]{\textnormal{d}}\alpha

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{V_{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}}}\left[\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}}}(V_{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}})\right]-{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\left[\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}(V_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}})\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{X_{t+1}}\left[\mathrm{Cov}_{X_{0}|X_{t+1}}(X_{t+1})\right]-{\mathbb{E}}_{X_{t}}\left[\mathrm{Cov}_{X_{0}|X_{t}}(X_{t})\right].

(137)

Next, in order to
further control the left-hand side of (
137
),
we proceed to bounding the difference between
\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}\,|\,V_{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}}}
and
\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}\,|\,V_{\alpha}}
for
\alpha\in[\overline{\alpha}_{t+1},\overline{\alpha}_{t}]
. Towards this end, we resort to the following SDE that describes the dynamics of the random process
\{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}\,|\,V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\}
, whose proof can be found in
Eldan (2022, Section 4.2.1)
:

{\textnormal{d}}\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})=-\frac{1}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}^{2}(V_{\alpha}){\textnormal{d}}\alpha+{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{(3)}{\textnormal{d}}B_{\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}}.

Here,
(B_{t})
denotes the standard Brownian motion in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and

{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{(l)}\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}\left[(V_{1}-{\mathbb{E}}[V_{1}|V_{\alpha}])^{\otimes l}\,|\,V_{\alpha}\right].

(138)

Denoting by
\langle M\rangle
the quadratic variation of a stochastic process
(M_{t})
, we can invoke Itô’s formula to obtain

\displaystyle{\textnormal{d}}\bigg(\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}^{2}(V_{\alpha})\right)\bigg)=2\left\langle\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha}),{\textnormal{d}}\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\right\rangle+{\textnormal{d}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\left(\left\langle\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}\right\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=2\left\langle\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha}),{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{(3)}{\textnormal{d}}B_{\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}}\right\rangle-\frac{2}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}\left\langle\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha}),\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}^{2}(V_{\alpha})\right\rangle{\textnormal{d}}\alpha

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\frac{1}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}\big\langle{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{(3)},{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{(3)}\big\rangle{\textnormal{d}}\alpha.

Taking expectation then yields

\displaystyle{\textnormal{d}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\left({\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}^{2}(V_{\alpha})\right]\right)\right]=-\frac{2}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha}),\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}^{2}(V_{\alpha})\right\rangle\right]{\textnormal{d}}\alpha

(139)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\big\langle{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{(3)},{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{(3)}\big\rangle\right]{\textnormal{d}}\alpha.

In order to control
{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}^{2}(V_{\alpha})\big]
through the differential equation (
139
), we need to bound the drift terms on the right-hand side of (
139
).
To bound the first term on the right-hand side of (
139
), we observe from the symmetry of
\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}\,|\,V_{\alpha}}
that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha}),\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}^{2}(V_{\alpha})\right\rangle\right]={\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}^{3}(V_{\alpha})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|\cdot\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\big(\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\big)\cdot\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\big(\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\big)\mathbbm{1}\{V_{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}\}\cdot\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\big(\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\big)\mathbbm{1}\{V_{\alpha}\notin{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}\}\cdot\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}(k\log T){\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\big(\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\big)\mathbbm{1}\{V_{\alpha}\notin{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}\}\cdot\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right],

(140)

where the last inequality follows from the definition of
{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}
in (
41
), Corollary
1
and the basic fact that

\mathrm{tr}\big(\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(v)\big)={\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\Big(\big(V_{1}-\mu_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}\big)\big(V_{1}-\mu_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}\big)^{\top}\Big)\,|\,V_{\alpha}=v\right]={\mathbb{E}}\left[\big\|V_{1}-\mu_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}\big\|^{2}\,|\,V_{\alpha}=v\right].

Regarding the last term of inequality (
140
), combining Assumption
1
and Lemma
1
results in

{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\big(\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\big)\mathbbm{1}\{V_{\alpha}\notin{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}\}\cdot\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]\leq 8T^{6c_{R}}{\mathbb{P}}(V_{\alpha}\notin{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha})\leq\frac{1}{T^{10}}.

As a consequence, we arrive at

{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha}),\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}^{2}(V_{\alpha})\right\rangle\right]\leq C_{3}\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}(k\log T){\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{T^{10}}.

(141)

Substitution into (
139
) yields

\displaystyle{\textnormal{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha}),\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}^{2}(V_{\alpha})\right\rangle\right]{\textnormal{d}}\alpha

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\big\langle{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{(3)},{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{(3)}\big\rangle\right]{\textnormal{d}}\alpha

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{2}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\langle\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha}),\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}^{2}(V_{\alpha})\right\rangle\right]{\textnormal{d}}\alpha

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{2}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}C_{3}\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}(k\log T){\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]{\textnormal{d}}\alpha-\frac{1}{T^{10}}{\textnormal{d}}\alpha

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{2C_{3}k\log T}{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]{\textnormal{d}}\alpha-\frac{1}{T^{10}}{\textnormal{d}}\alpha,

where the last line holds provided that
\alpha\in[\overline{\alpha}_{t+1},\overline{\alpha}_{t}]
.
In view of Grownwall’s inequality, we can derive

\displaystyle\exp\left\{\frac{2C_{3}k{\alpha}\log T}{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right\}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{{\alpha}}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]-\exp\left\{\frac{2C_{3}k\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}\log T}{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right\}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\big\|\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}}}(V_{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{1}{T^{10}}\int_{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}}^{{\alpha}}\exp\left\{\frac{2C_{3}k\alpha^{\prime}\log T}{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right\}{\textnormal{d}}\alpha^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{2C_{3}kT^{10}\log T}\left(\exp\left\{\frac{2C_{3}k{\alpha}\log T}{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right\}-\exp\left\{\frac{2C_{3}k\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}\log T}{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right\}\right).

Dividing both sides of the above inequality by
\exp\left\{\frac{2C_{3}k\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}\log T}{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right\}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\exp\left\{\frac{2C_{3}k(\alpha-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})\log T}{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right\}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}(V_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{2C_{3}kT^{10}\log T}\left(\exp\left\{\frac{2C_{3}k(\alpha-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})\log T}{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right\}-1\right).

According to Lemma
4
, every
\alpha\in[\overline{\alpha}_{t+1},\overline{\alpha}_{t}]
obeys

\frac{2C_{3}k(\alpha-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})\log T}{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\leq\frac{2C_{3}k(1-{\alpha}_{t+1})\log T}{{\alpha}_{t+1}-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}}\leq\frac{8C_{3}c_{1}k\log^{2}T}{T}\leq 1,

provided that
8C_{3}c_{1}k\log^{2}T\leq T
.
Consequently, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}}}(V_{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\bigg]\leq\exp\left\{\frac{2C_{3}k(\alpha-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})\log T}{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right\}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]

(142)

\displaystyle+\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{2C_{3}kT^{10}\log T}\left(\exp\left\{\frac{2C_{3}k(\alpha-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})\log T}{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right\}-1\right)

\displaystyle\leq 3{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]+\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{2C_{3}kT^{10}\log T}\left(\exp\left\{\frac{2C_{3}k(\alpha-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})\log T}{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right\}-1\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{\leq}3{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]+\frac{2(\overline{\alpha}_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})}{T^{10}},

where (a) holds since
e^{x}-1\leq 2x
for all
x\leq 1
.

To finish up, combining (
137
) and (
142
) and making use of the equivalence between
X_{t}
and
V_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}
give

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\mathrm{tr}

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{Cov}_{X_{0}|X_{t+1}}(X_{t+1})\right)\Big]-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathrm{Cov}_{X_{0}|X_{t}}(X_{t})\right)\right]=\int_{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}}^{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\frac{1}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathrm{Cov}^{2}_{V_{1}|V_{\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\right)\right]{\textnormal{d}}\alpha

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}}^{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\frac{1}{3(1-\alpha)^{2}}\left\{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}^{2}_{V_{1}|V_{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}}}(V_{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]-\frac{2(\overline{\alpha}_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})}{T^{10}}\right\}{\textnormal{d}}\alpha

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}(1-\alpha_{t+1})}{3(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{X_{0}|X_{t+1}}(X_{t+1})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]-\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}(1-\alpha_{t+1})^{2}}{T^{10}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\alpha_{t+1})}{3(\alpha_{t+1}-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{X_{0}|X_{t+1}}(X_{t+1})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]-\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}(1-\alpha_{t+1})^{2}}{T^{10}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})}.

Rearranging terms and recalling that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{t+1}^{2}=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\alpha_{t+1})}{(\alpha_{t+1}-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}_{t+1}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{X_{0}|X_{t+1}}(X_{t+1})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-\alpha_{t+1})\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}}{(\alpha_{t+1}-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\mathrm{Cov}_{X_{0}|X_{t+1}}(X_{t+1})}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]

(143)

\displaystyle\leq 3\left\{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}\big(\mathrm{Cov}_{X_{0}|X_{t+1}}(X_{t+1})\big)\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{tr}(\mathrm{Cov}_{X_{0}|X_{t}}(X_{t}))\right]\right\}+\frac{3}{T^{10}},

We have thus completed the proof of this lemma.

H.4
Proof of Lemma
4

A little algebra yields

\displaystyle\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}(1-\alpha_{t})}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}-

\displaystyle\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t+1}(1-\alpha_{t+1})}{2(\alpha_{t+1}-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})}=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}(1-\alpha_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})-\overline{\alpha}_{t}(1-\alpha_{t+1})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{\leq}\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}(1-\alpha_{t})[(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})-\alpha_{t}+\overline{\alpha}_{t}]}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})}=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}(1-\alpha_{t})[1-\alpha_{t}+\overline{\alpha}_{t}(1-\alpha_{t+1})]}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}(1-\alpha_{t})(1-\alpha_{t+1})}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t+1})}\leq\left(\frac{8c_{1}\log T}{T}\right)^{2}\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}.

Here, (a) follows since
1-\alpha_{t}\leq 1-\alpha_{t+1}
, while the last inequality applies (
45
).

H.5
Proof of Lemma
5

For any matrix
B
, we know that
B
and
B^{\top}
have the same determinant. As a result,

\displaystyle\log\det(I+\eta A+\eta\Delta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\{\log\det(I+\eta A+\eta\Delta^{\top})+\log\det(I+\eta A+\eta\Delta)\right\}

(144)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\log\det\left(I+2\eta A+\eta(\Delta^{\top}+\Delta)+\eta^{2}(A+\Delta)^{\top}(A+\Delta)\right).

For any vector
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, we make the observation that

\displaystyle x^{\top}\big(I+\eta(2A+\Delta^{\top}+\Delta)\big)x

\displaystyle=\left\|{x}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\eta x^{\top}Ax+\eta x^{\top}(\Delta^{\top}+\Delta)x

\displaystyle\geq\left\|{x}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\eta\left\|{\Delta^{\top}+\Delta}\right\|\left\|{x}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq(1-2\eta\left\|{\Delta}\right\|)\left\|{x}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\left\|{x}\right\|_{2}^{2},

thus implying that
I+2\eta A+\eta(\Delta^{\top}+\Delta)\succ 0
. Further, it is easily seen that

\displaystyle I+2\eta A+\eta(\Delta^{\top}+\Delta)\preceq I+2\eta A+\eta(\Delta^{\top}+\Delta)+\eta^{2}(A+\Delta)^{\top}(A+\Delta).

According to the Löwner–Heinz theorem,
\log A\preceq\log B
holds for any
0\preceq A\preceq B
.
This in turn allows one to derive

\displaystyle\log\det\big(I

\displaystyle+2\eta A+\eta(\Delta^{\top}+\Delta)+\eta^{2}(A+\Delta)^{\top}(A+\Delta)\big)

(145)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}\Big(\log\left(I+2\eta A+\eta(\Delta^{\top}+\Delta)+\eta^{2}(A+\Delta)^{\top}(A+\Delta)\right)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{tr}\Big(\log\big(I+2\eta A+\eta(\Delta^{\top}+\Delta)\big)\Big)=\log\det\big(I+2\eta A+\eta(\Delta^{\top}+\Delta)\big).

In addition, for any symmetric matrix
B\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
, we denote its eigenvalues as
\{\lambda_{i}(B)\}_{i=1}^{d}
, then Weyl’s inequality tells us that

\lambda_{i}\big(2\eta A+\eta(\Delta^{\top}+\Delta)\big)\geq 2\eta\lambda_{i}(A)-\eta\left\|{\Delta^{\top}+\Delta}\right\|\geq-2\eta\left\|{\Delta}\right\|\geq-\frac{1}{2}\qquad\text{for all }i\leq d.

Recalling the elementary inequality
\log(1+x)\geq x-x^{2}
for any
x\geq-1/2
, we can reach

\displaystyle\log\det\left(I+2\eta A+\eta(\Delta^{\top}+\Delta)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{d}\eta\lambda_{i}\left(2A+\Delta^{\top}+\Delta\right)-\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{d}\eta^{2}\lambda_{i}^{2}\left(2A+\Delta^{\top}+\Delta\right)

(146)

\displaystyle=\eta\mathrm{tr}\left(2A+\Delta^{\top}+\Delta\right)-\eta^{2}\left\|{2A+\Delta^{\top}+\Delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq 2\eta\mathrm{tr}(A)+2\eta\mathrm{tr}(\Delta)-8\eta^{2}\left\|{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-8\eta^{2}\left\|{\Delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

The proof can thus be completed by combining (
144
), (
145
) and (
146
).

Appendix I
Training guarantees adapted to low dimension

In this section, we discuss how the training step of diffusion models can also adapt to the intrinsic
dimension of the data distribution when a kernel-based score estimator is used.

We first define the following notation which will be appear
throughout this section.

•

k^{\star}\coloneqq C_{\mathsf{cover}}k
denotes the effective intrinsic
dimension appearing in the covering-number bounds.

•

For any
t\geq 0
, define

\gamma_{t}\coloneqq\sqrt{\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}
.

•

Throughout this section, the covering scale used in the definitions of

{\mathcal{I}}
,
{\mathcal{G}}
, and
{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}
from
(
36
), (
37
), and
(
41
) is taken as

\epsilon_{t}\coloneqq\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}
.
With this choice, the index set
{\mathcal{I}}
is defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}\coloneqq\left\{1\leq i\leq N_{\epsilon_{t}}:\mathbb{P}(X_{0}\in\mathcal{B}_{i})\geq\exp\left(-C_{1}k\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{\epsilon_{t}}\right)\right\}.

(147)

For notational simplicity, throughout this section, unless otherwise stated,
we write
N_{\varepsilon}
for
N_{\varepsilon}({\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}})
.

•

For
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, define

D(x)\coloneqq\sum_{r=1}^{n}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)
.

•

For
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, define the good-density event

{\mathcal{E}}_{x}\coloneqq\left\{\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)\geq\frac{1}{2}p_{X_{t}}(x)\right\}
.

•

For
y\in{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}}
and
0<\alpha<1
, define the posterior
typical set

{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(y)\coloneqq\left\{v\in{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}}:\sqrt{\alpha}\|v-y\|_{2}\leq 15\sqrt{k^{\star}(1-\alpha)\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\alpha}}\right\}.

(148)

•

For any measurable
f
, define the empirical posterior expectation induced
by the kernel weights

\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{0\mid t,x}[f(X_{0})]\coloneqq\frac{\sum_{r=1}^{n}f(X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}})\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}{D(x)}.

Further, define the corresponding empirical posterior mean by

\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)\coloneqq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{0\mid t,x}[X_{0}]
.

I.1
DDPM training complexity analysis (Proof of Theorem
6
)

We begin with the DDPM score-matching guarantee. The estimator is constructed
according to the relative size of
d
and

(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}
. We first define the soft-thresholding
function used in the low-dimensional ambient regime. Let

a_{t}\coloneqq C_{\mathsf{th}}\sqrt{d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}},

where
C_{\mathsf{th}}>0
is a sufficiently large universal constant,
and set
h_{t}:[0,\infty)\to[0,1]
as

h_{t}(r)\coloneqq\begin{cases}1,&0\leq r\leq a_{t},\\[1.99997pt]
0,&r\geq 2a_{t},\\[1.99997pt]
\left[1+\exp\left(\dfrac{2r/a_{t}-3}{(r/a_{t}-1)(2-r/a_{t})}\right)\right]^{-1},&a_{t}<r<2a_{t}.\end{cases}

Thus
h_{t}
is a decreasing soft-threshold function: it equals one on

[0,a_{t}]
, smoothly decays on
[a_{t},2a_{t}]
, and vanishes on

[2a_{t},\infty)
. In particular, since it is constant near the origin and
flat at the two transition endpoints, the radial map
x\mapsto h_{t}(\|x\|_{2})

is
C^{2}
.

For
z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, define the thresholded kernel used in the numerator by

\widetilde{\varphi}_{t,x}(z)\coloneqq\begin{cases}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}z-x\bigr),&d>(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{-1},\\[1.99997pt]
h_{t}\!\left(\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}z-x\|_{2}\right)\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}z-x\bigr),&d\leq(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}.\end{cases}

The score estimator is then defined by

s_{t}(x)\coloneq\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{i}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\bigr)\widetilde{\varphi}_{t,x}(X_{i}^{\mathsf{sample}})}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})D(x)}.

(149)

In the regime

d\leq(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}
, the prior concentration gives

\|s_{t}^{\star}(X_{t})\|_{2}=\widetilde{O}\big(\sqrt{d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}}\big)
with high
probability. If the sample size is not large enough, the raw estimator

\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-x
can be dominated by
large residuals; the cutoff above uses the prior scale and effectively shrinks
such unstable contributions toward zero.

We first collect several auxiliary estimates that will be used to prove the
score-matching guarantee. These lemmas isolate the density-ratio estimates,
typical-region reductions, and bad-event controls needed for the kernel
estimator analysis.

Lemma 21

Recall the definitions of
{\mathcal{I}},~{\mathcal{G}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(y)

in (
36
), (
37
)
and (
148
), respectively.
Then, for every
2\leq\beta\leq 6
, the contribution from the typical
region obeys the following upper bound:

\displaystyle\int_{y\in\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\int_{\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}}\int_{x_{0}\notin{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)}\varphi_{1}^{\beta}\big(\gamma_{t}(x_{0}-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}(\omega)\frac{p_{0}(x_{0})p_{0}(y)}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\beta d/2}p_{X_{t}}^{\beta}(x)}{\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y\leq\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}{n}.

Here
x=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\omega

and

\gamma_{t}=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}/(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}
.

The proof of Lemma
21
is deferred to
Section
I.5.1
.

Lemma 22

Under the same notation as Lemma
21
, define

\mathsf{S}(x_{0},v,y,\omega)\coloneqq\frac{\varphi_{1}^{2}\big(\gamma_{t}(x_{0}-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}\big(\gamma_{t}(v-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{0}(x_{0})p_{0}(y)p_{0}(v)}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{3d/2}p_{X_{t}}^{3}(x)},

where
x=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\omega
.
Then

\displaystyle\int_{y\in\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\int_{\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}}\int_{x_{0}}\int_{v\notin{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)}\mathsf{S}(x_{0},v,y,\omega){\textnormal{d}}v{\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y\leq\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}{n}.

The proof of Lemma
22
is deferred to
Section
I.5.2
.

Combining the preceding estimates with the construction in
(
149
), we obtain the following score-matching
guarantee.

Proposition 4
(Low-dimensional kernel score estimation)

Suppose Assumption
1
,
2
hold. Then the kernel-based score estimator
satisfies

\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}^{2}\;\lesssim\;\left(\min\left\{\frac{R_{0}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}},d\right\}\right)^{\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{n\,(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2+1}},

for all noise levels
t
in the regime considered.

The proof of Proposition
4
is deferred to
Section
I.3
.

Proof 1
(Proof of Theorem
6
)

By the definition of
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{ddpm}\text{-}\mathsf{sc}}
in
Assumption
4
and
Proposition
4
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathsf{ddpm}\text{-}\mathsf{sc}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\left(\min\left\{\frac{R_{0}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}},d\right\}\right)^{\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}.

Since
1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}\geq 1-\overline{\alpha}_{1}=1-\alpha_{1}=T^{-c_{0}}
, the logarithmic factor is bounded by

\log(dR_{0}nT^{c_{0}})
. Moreover,

\frac{\min\left\{\frac{R_{0}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}},d\right\}^{\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}\leq\min\left\{\frac{d^{\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}},\frac{R_{0}^{\frac{4}{k^{\star}+2}}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}}\right\}.

Since
\overline{\alpha}_{t}
is decreasing in
t
, the largest summand is
attained at
t=1
. Thus

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}}\leq\frac{1}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{1})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}}=\frac{1}{(1-\alpha_{1})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}}\lesssim T^{c_{0}\left(\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}\right)}.

Similarly,

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}\leq\frac{1}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{1})^{k^{\star}/2}}=\frac{1}{(1-\alpha_{1})^{k^{\star}/2}}\lesssim T^{c_{0}k^{\star}/2}.

Substituting these two estimates gives

\varepsilon_{\mathsf{ddpm}\text{-}\mathsf{sc}}^{2}\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\log(dR_{0}nT^{c_{0}})}{n}\min\left\{d^{\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}T^{c_{0}k^{\star}/2},R_{0}^{\frac{4}{k^{\star}+2}}T^{c_{0}\left(\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}\right)}\right\}.

Finally, since
1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}\leq 1
, the same upper bound controls

\varepsilon_{\mathsf{ddim}\text{-}\mathsf{sc}}^{2}=T^{-1}\sum_{t}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}^{2}
.

I.2
DDIM training complexity analysis (Proof of Theorem
3
)

In the DDIM setting, beyond score matching, we also need the higher-order
approximation guarantees required by Assumption
3
.
Because these smoothness requirements prevent us from using the construction in
(
149
), we instead use the simplest empirical
plug-in construction. Its precise form and guarantees are stated in the
following proposition.

Proposition 5
(Higher-order kernel approximation)

Suppose Assumption
1
,
2
hold. For the DDIM analysis, let

s_{t}
denote the unthresholded plug-in score estimator

s_{t}(x)\coloneqq\frac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{i}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\bigr)\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{i}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\bigr)}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{i}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\bigr)}.

Then the corresponding kernel posterior estimator satisfies the following
score and higher-order approximation bounds:

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{4}{k^{\star}+2}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+1+\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}}\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}},

(150a)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},1,t}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{16}{k^{\star}+4}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+2+\frac{8}{k^{\star}+4}}}\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}},

(150b)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},2,t}^{2}
