\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{16}{k^{\star}+4}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+2+\frac{8}{k^{\star}+4}}}\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}},

(150c)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Hess},t}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{3}R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{36}{k^{\star}+6}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+3+\frac{18}{k^{\star}+6}}}\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}.

(150d)

The proof of Proposition
5
is
deferred to Section
I.4
.

Proof 2
(Proof of Theorem
3
)

The proof follows directly from
Proposition
5
. We use

\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\leq\log(dR_{0}nT^{c_{0}})
. For the score
term, the definition of
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{ddim}\text{-}\mathsf{sc}}
gives

\varepsilon_{\mathsf{ddim}\text{-}\mathsf{sc}}^{2}\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{4}{k^{\star}+2}}\log^{2}(dR_{0}nT^{c_{0}})}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}}.

The average is bounded by the maximum summand, hence

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}}\leq\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{1}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{1})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}}=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{(1-\alpha_{1})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}}\lesssim T^{c_{0}\left(\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}\right)},

which yields the first claim.

For the two Jacobian terms, Assumption
3
averages
the pointwise errors with the weight
(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}
. Hence

\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},1}^{2}\vee\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},2}^{2}\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{16}{k^{\star}+4}}\log^{2}(dR_{0}nT^{c_{0}})}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+\frac{8}{k^{\star}+4}}}.

Again bounding the average by the maximum summand gives

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+\frac{8}{k^{\star}+4}}}\leq\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{1}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{1})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+\frac{8}{k^{\star}+4}}}=\frac{\alpha_{1}^{2}}{(1-\alpha_{1})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+\frac{8}{k^{\star}+4}}}\lesssim T^{c_{0}\left(\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+\frac{8}{k^{\star}+4}\right)}.

This gives the two Jacobian bounds.

Similarly, the Hessian term satisfies

\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Hess}}^{2}\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{36}{k^{\star}+6}}\log^{2}(dR_{0}nT^{c_{0}})}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{3}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+1+\frac{18}{k^{\star}+6}}}.

Finally, the same maximum-summand bound gives

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{3}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+1+\frac{18}{k^{\star}+6}}}\leq\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{1}^{3}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{1})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+1+\frac{18}{k^{\star}+6}}}=\frac{\alpha_{1}^{3}}{(1-\alpha_{1})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+1+\frac{18}{k^{\star}+6}}}\lesssim T^{c_{0}\left(\frac{k^{\star}}{2}+1+\frac{18}{k^{\star}+6}\right)}.

This gives the claimed Hessian bound.
Finally, we aim to cope with the last argument, i.e.,

\eta_{t}v^{\top}\nabla s_{t}(x)v\geq-\frac{1}{4}\|v\|_{2}^{2}.

To this end, let’s first apply Tweedie’s formula to
s_{t}(x)
(with the empirical density
\widehat{p}_{t}(\cdot)
as the potential distribution). Indeed, it can be derived that

\nabla s_{t}(x)=\nabla\left(\frac{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\frac{x}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)-\frac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}I.

Thus, for any
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, according to the semi-definite property of
\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)
, the following inequality holds for any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\eta_{t}^{\mathsf{ddim}}v^{\top}\nabla s_{t}(x)v

\displaystyle=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}\eta_{t}^{\mathsf{ddim}}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}v^{\top}\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)v-\frac{\eta_{t}^{\mathsf{ddim}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\|v\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{\eta_{t}^{\mathsf{ddim}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\|v\|_{2}^{2}\geq-\frac{1}{4}\|v\|_{2}^{2},

where the last inequality follows from the choice schedule of
\eta_{t}^{\mathsf{ddim}}
and (
45
).

I.3
Proof of Proposition
4

Recall that the unified estimator can be written as

s_{t}(x)=\frac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{i}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\right)\widetilde{\varphi}_{t,x}(X_{i}^{\mathsf{sample}})}{D(x)}.

For notational convenience in the decomposition below, define

\displaystyle\widehat{m}_{t}^{\rm th}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{i}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\right)\widetilde{\varphi}_{t,x}(X_{i}^{\mathsf{sample}})}{D(x)},

\displaystyle m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}}\!\left[\left(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x\right)\widetilde{\varphi}_{t,x}(X_{0})\right]}{p_{X_{t}}(x)},

\displaystyle m_{t}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0\mid t}(x)-x.

Then
s_{t}(x)=\widehat{m}_{t}^{\rm th}(x)/(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})
and
s_{t}^{\star}(x)=m_{t}(x)/(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})
. Hence, the score error first
can be split into the following three terms:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\|s_{t}(X_{t})-s_{t}^{\star}(X_{t})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|\widehat{m}_{t}^{\rm th}(X_{t})-m_{t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\notin{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|m_{t}^{\rm th}(X_{t})-m_{t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|\widehat{m}_{t}^{\rm th}(X_{t})-m_{t}^{\rm th}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\right].

(151)

Here
{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}
is the typical set
which is formulated at the begining of this section.
We first bound the first term in (
151
).
To this end, note that by the generate mechanism of
X_{t}
,

X_{t}=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}Y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Omega
, then it is easy to find that

\left\|\widehat{m}_{t}^{\rm th}(X_{t})-m_{t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}\lesssim R_{0}+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\|\Omega\|_{2}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|\widehat{m}_{t}^{\rm th}(X_{t})-m_{t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\notin{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 80.00012pt\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{Y,\Omega}\big[(R_{0}^{2}+(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\|\Omega\|_{2}^{2})\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\notin{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 80.00012pt\overset{\text{(a)}}{\lesssim}R_{0}^{2}{\mathbb{P}}(X_{t}\notin{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}})+d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}){\mathbb{P}}\Big(Y\notin\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\Big)+(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\mathbb{E}[\|\Omega\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{\Omega\notin{\mathcal{G}}\}]

\displaystyle\hskip 80.00012pt\lesssim(R_{0}^{2}+d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}))\left(\frac{\epsilon_{t}}{R_{0}}\right)^{4k^{\star}}\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}{n},

(152)

where (a) follows from the nature of Gaussian distribution and
the penultimate inequality holds by using
Lemma
1
. And at the last inequality we
use the choice of
\epsilon_{t}
.

To analyze the second term of (
151
)
, we need the following lemma.

Lemma 23

For any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}}\!\left[\left\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x\right\|_{2}^{2}\left(\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\bigr)-\widetilde{\varphi}_{t,x}(X_{0})\right)^{2}\right]}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}\leq\left(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{R_{0}n}\right)^{2d}.

In particular, for any
x\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}
, where

{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}
is defined in (
41
), it holds that

\displaystyle\|m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)-m_{t}(x)\|_{2}\leq\left(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{R_{0}n}\right)^{2d}.

The proof of Lemma
23
is deferred to
Section
I.5.3
.
Applying Lemma
23
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|m_{t}^{\rm th}(X_{t})-m_{t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{R_{0}n}\right)^{4d}\leq\frac{R_{0}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n^{2}}.

(153)

It remains to bound the third term. Note

{\mathcal{E}}_{x}=\{\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)\geq p_{X_{t}}(x)/2\}
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|\widehat{m}_{t}^{\rm th}(X_{t})-m_{t}^{\rm th}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt=\underbrace{\frac{1}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|\widehat{m}_{t}^{\rm th}(x)-m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}}\right]p_{X_{t}}(x){\textnormal{d}}x}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm good}}

\displaystyle\hskip 80.00012pt+\underbrace{\frac{1}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|\widehat{m}_{t}^{\rm th}(x)-m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}^{c}}\right]p_{X_{t}}(x){\textnormal{d}}x}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad}}.

(154)

In the sequel, we bound
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm good}
and
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad}
separately.

Bound for
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm good}
.

For the thresholded empirical fluctuation, first write

\displaystyle\widehat{m}_{t}^{\rm th}(x)-m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{D(x)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigl[\left(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{i}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\right)\widetilde{\varphi}_{t,x}(X_{i}^{\mathsf{sample}})

\displaystyle\hskip 70.0001pt-m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{i}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)\Bigr].

(155)

For each fixed
x
, the summands in
(
155
) are centered:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}}\!\left[\left(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x\right)\widetilde{\varphi}_{t,x}(X_{0})-m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\bigr)\right]=0.

(156)

Thus, on
{\mathcal{E}}_{x}
, we first lower bound the denominator
D(x)
by

np_{X_{t}}(x)/2
and drop the indicator:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|\widehat{m}_{t}^{\rm th}(x)-m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}}\right]\leq\frac{4}{p_{X_{t}}(x)^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigl[\left(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{i}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\right)\widetilde{\varphi}_{t,x}(X_{i}^{\mathsf{sample}})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 130.0002pt\left.\left.-m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{i}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)\Bigr]\right\|_{2}^{2}\right].

(157)

By the zero-mean property in
(
156
) and independence of the training
samples, the last expectation equals the variance of one summand divided by

n
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|\widehat{m}_{t}^{\rm th}(x)-m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{4}{np_{X_{t}}(x)^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}}\!\left[\left\|\left(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x\right)\widetilde{\varphi}_{t,x}(X_{0})-m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\bigr)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{1}{np_{X_{t}}(x)^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}}\!\left[\left\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x\right\|_{2}^{2}\left(\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\bigr)-\widetilde{\varphi}_{t,x}(X_{0})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{np_{X_{t}}(x)^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}}\!\left[\left\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x-m_{t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\bigr)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{np_{X_{t}}(x)^{2}}\left\|m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)-m_{t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}}\!\left[\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\bigr)\right].

(158)

Notice that (
158
) holds for
every
x
. We now take the expectation on both sides of
(
158
) over

X_{t}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}
.
For the first term on the right hand side of
(
158
),
Lemma
23
gives

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\frac{1}{np_{X_{t}}(x)}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}}\!\left[\left\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x\right\|_{2}^{2}\left(\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\bigr)-\widetilde{\varphi}_{t,x}(X_{0})\right)^{2}\right]{\textnormal{d}}x\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}{n};

(159)

For the third term on the right hand side of
(
158
), we additionally use the
following density-ratio estimate.

Lemma 24

Suppose the intrinsic dimension assumption holds. Then, for every noise level

t
in the regime considered, the following term satisfies

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\bigr)p_{0}(x_{0})}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}{\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}x\leq C\left(\frac{R_{0}\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\right)^{k^{\star}}.

The proof of Lemma
24
is deferred to
Section
I.5.4
.
Combining Lemma
23
and
Lemma
24
gives

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\frac{\left\|m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)-m_{t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}}{np_{X_{t}}(x)}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}}\!\left[\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\bigr)\right]{\textnormal{d}}x\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}{n}.

(160)

For the second term of the right hand side of
(
158
),
note that we have

\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0})}{p_{X_{t}}^{2}(x)}p_{X_{0}}(X_{0})=\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0})}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}p_{0\mid t}(X_{0}\mid x).

Combining the definition of
m_{t}(x)
and Jensen’s inequality yields

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}np_{X_{t}}(x)}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}}\!\left[\left\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x-m_{t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\bigr)\right]{\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{X_{t}}\bigg[\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{Z\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\bigg[\|Z-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{2}^{2}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}Z\bigr)}{p_{X_{t}}(X_{t})}\bigg]\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\underbrace{\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{Y,\Omega}\bigg[\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{Z\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\bigg[\|Z-Y\|_{2}^{2}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}Z\bigr)}{p_{X_{t}}(X_{t})}\bigg]\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\bigg]}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{J}}_{1}}

(161)

\displaystyle+\frac{8}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\underbrace{\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{Y,\Omega}\bigg[\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\bigl[\|Y-V\|_{2}^{2}\bigr]\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{Z\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\bigg[\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}Z\bigr)}{p_{X_{t}}(X_{t})}\bigg]\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\bigg]}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{J}}_{2}}.

Here
Y\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{X_{0}}
,
\Omega\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,I_{d})
, and

X_{t}=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\,Y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\,\Omega
. The
last step uses
\|Z-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\|Z-Y\|_{2}^{2}+2\|Y-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{2}^{2}
, as well as Jensen’s
inequality

\|Y-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}[\|Y-V\|_{2}^{2}]
.

To analyze the two terms
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
above, we need the following
lemmas.
In the next two lemmas,
A\lesssim_{\beta}B
means that

A\leq C_{\beta}B
for a constant
C_{\beta}
depending only on the fixed
integer
\beta
and on universal constants.

Lemma 25

Let
\beta\geq 2
be an integer. Recall the generative process of
X_{t}
,
i.e.,
X_{t}=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\,Y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\,\Omega
, where
Y\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{X_{0}}
and

\Omega\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,I_{d})
. Define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{1,\beta}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Y,\Omega}\bigg[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Z\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\bigg[\|Z-Y\|_{2}^{\beta}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}Z\bigr)}{p_{X_{t}}(X_{t})}\bigg]\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\bigg].

Then

{\mathcal{J}}_{1,\beta}\lesssim_{\beta}\left(k^{\star}\log\frac{R_{0}nd}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right)^{\beta/2}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\gamma_{t}^{k^{\star}-\beta}\;+\;\frac{R_{0}^{\beta}\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{\beta}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\beta}}{n}.

The proof of Lemma
25
is deferred to
Section
I.5.5
.

Lemma 26

Under the same notation as Lemma
25
, define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{2,\beta}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Y,\Omega}\bigg[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\bigl[\|Y-V\|_{2}^{\beta}\bigr]\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Z\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\bigg[\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}Z\bigr)}{p_{X_{t}}(X_{t})}\bigg]\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\bigg].

Then

{\mathcal{J}}_{2,\beta}\lesssim_{\beta}\left(k^{\star}\log\frac{R_{0}nd}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right)^{\beta/2}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\gamma_{t}^{k^{\star}-\beta}\;+\;\frac{R_{0}^{\beta}\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{\beta}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\beta}}{n}.

The proof of Lemma
26
is deferred to
Section
I.5.6
.

By Lemmas
25
and
26
with
\beta=2
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k^{\star}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2-1}}+\frac{R_{0}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}{n};

(162a)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k^{\star}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2-1}}+\frac{R_{0}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}{n}.

(162b)

Combining
(
158
)–(
162
),
we get

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm good}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k^{\star}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{n\overline{\alpha}_{t}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2+1}}+\frac{R_{0}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}{n}.

(163)

Bound for
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad}
.

Set

\rho_{t}>0\quad\text{to be chosen later}
.
For
x=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\omega
,
we can write the following decomposition,

\widehat{m}^{\rm th}_{t}(x)-m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)={\mathcal{A}}_{t}(y,\omega)+{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(y,\omega)+{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(y,\omega),

where the three terms are defined as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{t}(y,\omega)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{D(x)}\sum_{r=1}^{n}\bigg\{\Big((\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x)h_{t}(\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\|_{2})-m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)\Big){\varphi}_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt\times\mathbbm{1}\{X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\in{\mathcal{B}}(y,\rho_{t})\}\mathbbm{1}\{\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\in{\mathcal{B}}(y,\rho_{t}\wedge a_{t})\}\bigg\};

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(y,\omega)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{D(x)}\sum_{r=1}^{n}\bigg\{\Big((\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x)h_{t}(\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\|_{2})-m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)\Big){\varphi}_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt\times\mathbbm{1}\{X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\notin{\mathcal{B}}(y,\rho_{t})\}\mathbbm{1}\{\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\in{\mathcal{B}}(y,\rho_{t}\wedge a_{t})\}\bigg\};

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(y,\omega)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{D(x)}\bigg\{\sum_{r=1}^{n}\Big((\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x)h_{t}(\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\|_{2})-m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)\Big){\varphi}_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt\times\mathbbm{1}\{\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\notin{\mathcal{B}}(y,\rho_{t}\wedge a_{t})\}.

Here
{\mathcal{B}}(y,r)
denotes the closed
\ell_{2}
-neighborhood
defined in the notation paragraph, and should not be confused with

{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(y,\omega)
above.
By the triangle inequality, the bad term decomposes as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad}

\displaystyle\lesssim\underbrace{\frac{1}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\|{\mathcal{A}}_{t}(y,\omega)\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}^{c}}\right]\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad,A}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\frac{1}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\|{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(y,\omega)\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}^{c}}\right]\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad,B}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\frac{1}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\left[\|{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(y,\omega)\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}^{c}}\right]\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad,C}}.

(165)

We first bound
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad,A}
. Since
on the support of every summand, we have
h_{t}(\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\|_{2})=1
. Hence

\displaystyle\left\|{{\mathcal{A}}_{t}(y,\omega)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\left\|{X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y}\right\|_{2}+\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\left\|{y-\mu_{0t}(x)}\right\|_{2}+\|m_{t}(x)-m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\rho_{t}+\left(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{R_{0}n}\right)^{2d}\leq 3\rho_{t},

(166)

where the penultimate inequality holds by using
Lemma
23
and the last inequality holds
as long as
\rho_{t}\geq\left(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{R_{0}n}\right)^{2d}
.
Further, we use the following lemma, which controls the event where the
empirical density deviates significantly from its population counterpart.

Lemma 27

Suppose the intrinsic dimension assumption holds. Then, for every noise level
t
in the
regime considered,

\displaystyle\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{P}\!\left(\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)<\frac{1}{2}p_{X_{t}}(x)\right)\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}},

where
x=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\omega
.

The proof of Lemma
27
is deferred to
Section
I.5.7
.

Combining (
166
) with
Lemma
27
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad,A}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\rho_{t}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{P}\!\left(\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)<\frac{1}{2}p_{X_{t}}(x)\right)\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\rho_{t}^{2}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2+2}},

(167)

We next turn to
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad,B}
.
Note that given
\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\|_{2}\leq 2a_{t}
,
\|\mu_{0\mid t}(x)-y\|_{2}\leq a_{t}

and
\left\|{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y-x}\right\|_{2}\leq a_{t}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\Big\|\big(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\big)h_{t}(\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\|_{2})-m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 80.00012pt\leq\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\|_{2}+\left\|{m_{t}(x)-m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 110.00017pt+\left\|{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y-x-m_{t}(x)}\right\|_{2}+\left\|{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y-x}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 80.00012pt\leq 3a_{t}+\rho_{t}\wedge a_{t}+\left(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{R_{0}n}\right)^{2d}\leq 5a_{t}.

(168)

Further, according to the definition of the threshold
function
h_{t}(\cdot)
,
h_{t}(\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\|_{2})=0
if
\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\|_{2}>2a_{t}
. And for
x\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}
,
\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y-x\|_{2}=\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\|\omega\|_{2}\leq a_{t}
.
This means all the conditions which guarantee
(
168
) hold. Now, define

\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)\coloneqq\frac{\sum_{r=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}\{\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}{D(x)}.

We have

\|{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(y,\omega)\|_{2}\leq 5a_{t}\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)
.
For the empirical ratio
\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)
, we have the following
result to help bound it.

Lemma 28

Let
x=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\omega
and

\gamma_{t}=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}/(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}
. Let

\{{\mathcal{C}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N_{\lambda_{t}}}
be a measurable partition induced by a

\lambda_{t}
-net of
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}}
, and suppose

0<\lambda_{t}\leq\frac{\rho_{t}}{2},~\rho_{t}\geq 32\gamma_{t}^{-1}\sqrt{\log\frac{R_{0}nd}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}}.

Define

\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)\coloneqq\frac{\sum_{r=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}\{\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}{D(x)}.

Then

\displaystyle\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)^{2}\right]\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y\lesssim\frac{N_{\lambda_{t}}}{n}+\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{6}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{6}}{n^{2}}.

The proof of Lemma
28
is deferred to
Section
I.5.8
.

Applying Lemma
28
makes us obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad,B}

\displaystyle\lesssim a_{t}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{Y,\Omega}[\mathsf{R}_{t}(Y,\Omega)]\lesssim\frac{a_{t}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\frac{1}{n}\left(\frac{R_{0}}{\lambda_{t}}\right)^{k^{\star}}+\frac{R_{0}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}{n}.

(169)

Finally, for
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad,C}
, using Lemma
2

leads to

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad,C}

\displaystyle\lesssim dR_{0}^{2}\,{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|Y-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\wedge a_{t}\mid{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\big)\leq dR_{0}^{2}\left(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{R_{0}n}\right)^{4k^{\star}}\leq\frac{R_{0}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n^{2}}.

(170)

Here the second inequality holds provided that
\rho_{t}\gtrsim\sqrt{k(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}
.
We now take
\lambda_{t}=\rho_{t}/2
and combine
(
167
) – (
170
).
This gives

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad,A}+{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad,B}+{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad,C}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\rho_{t}^{2}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2+2}}+\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}a_{t}^{2}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}\rho_{t}^{k^{\star}}}+\frac{k^{\star}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{n\overline{\alpha}_{t}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2+1}}+\frac{R_{0}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n^{2}}.

(171)

The first two terms are minimized by balancing them:

\displaystyle\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\rho_{t}^{2}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2+2}}\asymp\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}a_{t}^{2}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}\rho_{t}^{k^{\star}}}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\rho_{t}^{k^{\star}+2}\asymp a_{t}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}.

(172)

According to this, we choose that

\rho_{t}\asymp k^{\star}a_{t}^{\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/(2(k^{\star}+2))}
.
Substituting this choice into the algebraic part of
(
171
) and integrating with
the definition of
a_{t}
yields

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm bad}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{a_{t}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2+1}}+\frac{k^{\star}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{n\overline{\alpha}_{t}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2+1}}+\frac{R_{0}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\left\{\frac{R_{0}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}},d\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right\}^{\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}\frac{k^{\star}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2+1}}+\frac{R_{0}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n^{2}}.

(173)

Substituting (
163
) and
(
173
) into (
154
),
and then using (
153
), gives the current
combined bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|s_{t}(X_{t})-s_{t}^{\star}(X_{t})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\left\{\frac{R_{0}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}},d\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right\}^{\frac{2}{k^{\star}+2}}\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2+1}}.

(174)

I.4
Proof of Proposition
5

We first rewrite the approximation errors in
Assumption
3
in terms of posterior moment
estimation. To avoid repeating the score-matching argument at several moment
orders, we prove the following general posterior mean approximation bound.

Lemma 29

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold. For
the unthresholded empirical posterior mean

\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)\coloneqq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{0\mid t,x}[X_{0}],

the following bound holds for every
1\leq\beta\leq 3
and every noise level

t
in the regime considered:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2\beta}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{4\beta^{2}}{k^{\star}+2\beta}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}-\frac{\beta k^{\star}}{k^{\star}+2\beta}}}\log^{\beta}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}+\frac{R_{0}^{2\beta}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2\beta}}{n^{2}}.

(175)

The proof of Lemma
29
is deferred to
Section
I.5.9
.

Taking
\beta=1
in Lemma
29
and using
Tweedie’s formula yields (
150a
). It remains to
prove the higher-order bounds.

I.4.1
Frobenius-Norm Jacobian Approximation

The Frobenius-norm Jacobian error is a posterior covariance estimation
error. Define

\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)\coloneqq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{0\mid t,x}\!\left[\left(X_{0}-\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\left(X_{0}-\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)\right)^{\top}\right]

and let
\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)
denote the corresponding population posterior
covariance.
By Tweedie’s formula (
46
) and the unthresholded
definition of
s_{t}
, the kernel score estimator satisfies

s_{t}(x)-s_{t}^{\star}(x)=\frac{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\left(\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right).

Differentiating the two Tweedie representations gives

\frac{\partial s_{t}(x)}{\partial x}-\frac{\partial s_{t}^{\star}(x)}{\partial x}=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)\right).

(176)

Therefore the first Jacobian error in
Assumption
3
can be formulated as

\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},1,t}^{2}=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left\|\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right].

(177)

Thus it remains to control the covariance-error term in
(
177
).
For the Frobenius bound, we center the empirical covariance at the population
posterior mean:

\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)\coloneqq\frac{\sum_{r=1}^{n}\left(X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\left(X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)^{\top}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}{D(x)}.

(178)

Then

\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)=\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)-\left(\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\left(\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)^{\top},

and hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\left[\left\|\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\left[\left\|\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\left[\left\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{4}\right].

(179)

We first analyze the first term in (
179
), namely

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}[\|\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}]
.
We first split according to the typical region:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\left[\left\|\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]p_{X_{t}}(x){\textnormal{d}}x+R_{0}^{4}\mathbb{P}\!\left(X_{t}\notin{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right),

(180)

where the last term is controlled by Lemma
1
and is
absorbed into the final bound. It remains to control the first term on the
right-hand side of (
180
). To this end, we
decompose according to whether

{\mathcal{E}}_{x}\coloneqq\{\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)\geq p_{X_{t}}(x)/2\}
holds:

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]p_{X_{t}}(x){\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\leq\underbrace{\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\left[\left\|\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}}\right]\!p_{X_{t}}(x){\textnormal{d}}x}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm good}}

\displaystyle\hskip 70.0001pt+\underbrace{\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\left[\left\|\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}^{c}}\right]\!p_{X_{t}}(x){\textnormal{d}}x}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm bad}}.

(181)

We first bound
{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm good}
. Write

X_{t}=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\omega
with

Y\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{X_{0}}
and
\Omega\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,I_{d})
. For fixed
x
, absorb

\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)
into each numerator summand:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left\{\left(X_{0}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\left(X_{0}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)^{\top}-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)\right\}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\bigr)\right]=0.

(182)

On
{\mathcal{E}}_{x}
, the denominator can be replaced by
p_{X_{t}}(x)/2
. By
(
182
), the numerator summands are centered,
so the cross terms vanish by independence. Hence, using the posterior density,

{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm good}\lesssim\frac{1}{n}\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Y,\Omega}\left[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Z\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\left[\|Z-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{2}^{4}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}Z\bigr)}{p_{X_{t}}(X_{t})}\right]\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\right].

For
V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})
, Jensen’s inequality gives

\|Z-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{2}^{4}\lesssim\|Z-Y\|_{2}^{4}+\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\left[\|V-Y\|_{2}^{4}\right].

Hence

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm good}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n}\underbrace{\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Y,\Omega}\left[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Z\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\left[\|Z-Y\|_{2}^{4}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}Z\bigr)}{p_{X_{t}}(X_{t})}\right]\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\right]}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{L}}_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}\underbrace{\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Y,\Omega}\left[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\left[\|V-Y\|_{2}^{4}\right]\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Z\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\left[\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}Z\bigr)}{p_{X_{t}}(X_{t})}\right]\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\right]}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{L}}_{2}}.

(183)

The two terms in (
183
) are exactly the
quantities controlled by Lemmas
25
and

26
with
\beta=4
. Hence

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(k^{\star})^{2}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2-2}}+\frac{R_{0}^{4}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n}.

(184a)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(k^{\star})^{2}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2-2}}+\frac{R_{0}^{4}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n},

(184b)

Combining
(
183
), (
184a
), and
(
184b
) yields

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm good}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(k^{\star})^{2}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{n\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2-2}}+\frac{R_{0}^{4}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n}.

(185)

We next bound
{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm bad}
.
For
x=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\omega
, define

\widehat{Q}_{t}(y,\omega)\coloneqq\frac{\sum_{r=1}^{n}\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}{D(x)},\qquad m_{t}(y,\omega)\coloneqq\mathbb{E}_{V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid x)}\left[\|V-y\|_{2}^{2}\right].

We use the following deterministic bound. Since

\sum_{r}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}})=D(x)
, we may write

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{\sum_{r=1}^{n}\left[\left(X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\left(X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)^{\top}-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)\right]\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}{D(x)}.

Thus, using
\|aa^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\|a\|_{2}^{2}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{r=1}^{n}\left\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}{D(x)}+\left\|\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{Q}_{t}(y,\omega)+\left\|\mu_{0\mid t}(x)-y\right\|_{2}^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid x)}\left[\left\|V-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{\lesssim}\widehat{Q}_{t}(y,\omega)+2\left\|\mu_{0\mid t}(x)-y\right\|_{2}^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid x)}\left[\left\|V-y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(b)}}{\lesssim}\widehat{Q}_{t}(y,\omega)+m_{t}(y,\omega).

(186)

Here (a) follows from

\|V-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\|V-y\|_{2}^{2}+\|y-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2}
, and (b) holds since

\|\mu_{0\mid t}(x)-y\|_{2}^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}_{V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid x)}\left[\|V-y\|_{2}^{2}\right]

by Jensen’s inequality. Therefore, writing

\rho_{t}>0
for a truncation radius to be chosen later,

\widehat{Q}_{t}(y,\omega)={\mathcal{A}}_{t}(y,\omega)+{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(y,\omega),

where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{t}(y,\omega)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\sum_{r=1}^{n}\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}\leq\rho_{t}\}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}{D(x)}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(y,\omega)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\sum_{r=1}^{n}\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}{D(x)}.

By the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm bad}

\displaystyle\lesssim\underbrace{\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{E}\!\left[{\mathcal{A}}_{t}(y,\omega)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}^{c}}\right]\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm bad,A}}+\underbrace{\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{E}\!\left[{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(y,\omega)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}^{c}}\right]\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm bad,B}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\int_{y,\omega}m_{t}(y,\omega)^{2}\mathbb{P}\!\left(\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)<\frac{1}{2}p_{X_{t}}(x)\right)\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm bad,M}}.

(188)

Since
{\mathcal{A}}_{t}(y,\omega)\leq\rho_{t}^{2}
, Lemma
27

gives

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm bad,A}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\rho_{t}^{4}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}.

(189)

For the tail empirical part, introduce a covering scale
\lambda_{t}>0
. To
apply Lemma
28
, we require

0<\lambda_{t}\leq\frac{\rho_{t}}{2},\qquad\rho_{t}\geq 32\sqrt{\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\,\sqrt{\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}.

Define
\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)
as in Lemma
28
. Since

{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(y,\omega)\leq R_{0}^{2}\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)
, applying
Lemma
28
and

N_{\lambda_{t}}\leq(R_{0}/\lambda_{t})^{k^{\star}}
yields

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm bad,B}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{4}}{n}\left(\frac{R_{0}}{\lambda_{t}}\right)^{k^{\star}}+\frac{R_{0}^{4}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{6}}{n^{2}}.

(190)

We finally bound
{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm bad,M}
. Recall

m_{t}(y,\omega)=\mathbb{E}_{V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid x)}\left[\|V-y\|_{2}^{2}\right].

Recall the definition of
{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)
from
the very begining of this section.
Splitting the posterior expectation according to whether

V\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)
, we get

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm bad,M}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{y,\omega}\left(\mathbb{E}_{V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid x)}\left[\|V-y\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{V\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)\}\right]\right)^{2}\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{E}}_{x}^{c})\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{y,\omega}\left(\mathbb{E}_{V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid x)}\left[\|V-y\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{V\notin{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)\}\right]\right)^{2}\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{E}}_{x}^{c})\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y.

(191)

For the first term in (
191
), the definition of

{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)
gives

\displaystyle\int_{y,\omega}\left(\mathbb{E}_{V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid x)}\left[\|V-y\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{V\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)\}\right]\right)^{2}\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{E}}_{x}^{c})\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\left(\frac{k^{\star}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{2}\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{P}\!\left(\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)<\frac{1}{2}p_{X_{t}}(x)\right)\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}\left(\frac{k^{\star}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{2},

(192)

where the last step uses Lemma
27
.
For the second term in (
191
), use the support bound and discard

\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{E}}_{x}^{c})
:

\displaystyle\int_{y,\omega}\left(\mathbb{E}_{V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid x)}\left[\|V-y\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{V\notin{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)\}\right]\right)^{2}\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{E}}_{x}^{c})\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim R_{0}^{4}\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{E}_{V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid x)}\left[\mathbbm{1}\{V\notin{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)\}\right]\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle=R_{0}^{4}\mathbb{P}\!\left(V\notin{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(X_{0})\right)\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{4}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n^{2}},

(193)

where
V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})
, and the last step follows from
Lemma
2
. Combining
(
191
), (
192
), and
(
193
) gives

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm bad,M}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}\left(\frac{k^{\star}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{2}+\frac{R_{0}^{4}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n^{2}}.

(194)

Combining (
188
),
(
189
), (
190
), and
(
194
), we have, under the conditions of
Lemmas
27
and
28
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm bad}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\rho_{t}^{4}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}+\frac{R_{0}^{4}}{n}\left(\frac{R_{0}}{\lambda_{t}}\right)^{k^{\star}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}\left(\frac{k^{\star}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{2}+\frac{R_{0}^{4}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n^{2}}.

(195)

We now take
\lambda_{t}=\rho_{t}/2
. Balancing the local and tail empirical
terms, choose

\rho_{t}\asymp k^{\star}R_{0}^{\frac{4}{k^{\star}+4}}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2(k^{\star}+4)}}\sqrt{\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}},\qquad\lambda_{t}=\frac{\rho_{t}}{2}.

With this choice, the second and third terms in
(
195
) are absorbed by the local empirical
term. Substituting into (
195
) yields

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm bad}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k^{\star}R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{16}{k^{\star}+4}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}-\frac{2k^{\star}}{k^{\star}+4}}}\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}+\frac{R_{0}^{4}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n^{2}}.

(196)

Combining (
181
),
(
185
), and (
196
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\left[\left\|\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{k^{\star}R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{16}{k^{\star}+4}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}-\frac{2k^{\star}}{k^{\star}+4}}}\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}+\frac{R_{0}^{4}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n^{2}}.

(197)

The mean term in (
179
) is exactly the

\beta=2
case of Lemma
29
. Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\left[\left\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{16}{k^{\star}+4}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}-\frac{2k^{\star}}{k^{\star}+4}}}\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}+\frac{R_{0}^{4}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n^{2}}.

(198)

Finally, (
179
), (
197
),
and (
198
) give, through
(
177
),

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},1,t}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}\bigg[\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{16}{k^{\star}+4}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}-\frac{2k^{\star}}{k^{\star}+4}}}\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}+\frac{R_{0}^{4}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n^{2}}\bigg].

(199)

I.4.2
Trace Jacobian Approximation

The DDIM integration-by-parts argument uses the square of the trace error,
not the trace of the squared error matrix. Hence the second Jacobian error
should be read as

\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},2,t}^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left(\mathrm{tr}\left(\frac{\partial s_{t}(X_{t})}{\partial x}-\frac{\partial s_{t}^{\star}(X_{t})}{\partial x}\right)\right)^{2}\right].

Using Tweedie’s formula (
46
) and its empirical
counterpart for
s_{t}
, this becomes

\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},2,t}^{2}=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left(\mathrm{tr}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right)\right)^{2}\right].

(200)

We use the same centered covariance decomposition as above. Since

\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)\right)=\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)\right)-\left\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2},

we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left(\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left(\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right)\right)^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{4}\right].

(201)

It remains to control the centered trace-covariance term. The proof is the
scalar analogue of the Frobenius argument above. On the good event

{\mathcal{E}}_{x}=\{\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)\geq p_{X_{t}}(x)/2\}
, absorb

\mathrm{tr}(\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x))
into each numerator summand:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left\{\left\|X_{0}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}-\mathrm{tr}(\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x))\right\}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\bigr)\right]=0.

(202)

Thus the denominator is bounded below by
p_{X_{t}}(x)/2
, and the cross terms
vanish by independence. Hence the good-event contribution is bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left(\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right)\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{X_{t}}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{1}{n}\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Y,\Omega}\left[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Z\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\left[\left(\left\|Z-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}-\mathrm{tr}(\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t}))\right)^{2}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}Z\bigr)}{p_{X_{t}}(X_{t})}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n}\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Y,\Omega}\left[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Z\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\left[\left\|Z-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{4}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}Z\bigr)}{p_{X_{t}}(X_{t})}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Y,\Omega}\left[\left(\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Z\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\left[\left\|Z-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\right)^{2}\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Z\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\left[\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}Z\bigr)}{p_{X_{t}}(X_{t})}\right]\right].

(203)

By Jensen, both displayed terms are bounded by the two fourth-moment quantities
estimated in (
183
)–(
185
).
On the bad event, the deterministic estimate used for the Frobenius bound also
applies to the trace:

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{r=1}^{n}\left|\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2}-\mathrm{tr}(\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x))\right|\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}{D(x)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{Q}_{t}(y,\omega)+m_{t}(y,\omega),

(204)

where
x=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\omega
, and

\widehat{Q}_{t},m_{t}
are the same quantities used in
(
186
). Therefore the same

\widehat{Q}_{t}={\mathcal{A}}_{t}+{\mathcal{B}}_{t}
decomposition, with the same optimized

\rho_{t},\lambda_{t}
, gives the same final estimate as in the Frobenius case:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left(\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{k^{\star}R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{16}{k^{\star}+4}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}-\frac{2k^{\star}}{k^{\star}+4}}}\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}+\frac{R_{0}^{4}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n^{2}}.

(205)

Finally, (
201
),
(
205
), and (
198
) imply

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},2,t}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}\bigg[\frac{k^{\star}R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{16}{k^{\star}+4}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}-\frac{2k^{\star}}{k^{\star}+4}}}\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}+\frac{R_{0}^{4}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{4}}{n^{2}}\bigg].

(206)

I.4.3
Trace-Hessian Approximation

We start by reformulating
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Hess},t}^{2}
under the
unthresholded kernel estimator
s_{t}
defined in
Proposition
5
:

\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Hess},t}^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left\|\nabla\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\frac{\partial s_{t}(X_{t})}{\partial x}-\frac{\partial s_{t}^{\star}(X_{t})}{\partial x}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right].

By differentiating Tweedie’s formula (
46
) and its
empirical counterpart for
s_{t}
,

\displaystyle\nabla\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\frac{\partial s_{t}(x)}{\partial x}-\frac{\partial s_{t}^{\star}(x)}{\partial x}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\nabla\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)\right).

(207)

It remains to compute the trace-covariance gradient. Since

\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)\right)=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{0\mid t,x}\!\left[\|X_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\right]-\left\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2},

differentiating the normalized Gaussian weights gives

\displaystyle\nabla\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{0\mid t,x}\!\left[\|X_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\right]=\frac{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{0\mid t,x}\!\left[\left(X_{0}-\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\|X_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\right],\quad\nabla\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)=\frac{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x).

Therefore

\displaystyle\nabla\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{0\mid t,x}\left[\left(X_{0}-\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\left\|X_{0}-\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right],

(208)

where the last equality is the algebraic simplification after subtracting

\nabla\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2}=2(\nabla\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x))\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)
. This computation
identifies the empirical third central moment

\widehat{M}_{3,0\mid t}(x)\coloneqq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{0\mid t,x}\left[\left(X_{0}-\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\left\|X_{0}-\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right],

and, analogously, its population counterpart

M_{3,0\mid t}(x)\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left(X_{0}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\left\|X_{0}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\mid X_{t}=x\right].

Hence

\nabla\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)\right)=\frac{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\widehat{M}_{3,0\mid t}(x),\qquad\nabla\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)\right)=\frac{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}M_{3,0\mid t}(x).

Substituting these two identities into
(
207
), we get

\displaystyle\nabla\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\frac{\partial s_{t}(x)}{\partial x}-\frac{\partial s_{t}^{\star}(x)}{\partial x}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\nabla\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\widehat{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mathrm{Cov}_{0\mid t}(x)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{3/2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{3}}\left(\widehat{M}_{3,0\mid t}(x)-M_{3,0\mid t}(x)\right).

(209)

Consequently, the Hessian approximation error is exactly

\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Hess},t}^{2}=\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{3}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{6}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left\|\widehat{M}_{3,0\mid t}(X_{t})-M_{3,0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right].

(210)

Thus it remains to control the third-central-moment error in
(
210
).
As before, we first center the empirical moment at the population posterior
mean. Define

\widetilde{M}_{3,0\mid t}(x)\coloneqq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{0\mid t,x}\!\left[\left(X_{0}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\left\|X_{0}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right].

We now expand
\widehat{M}_{3,0\mid t}(x)
around the population posterior
centering. Set

\delta_{x}\coloneqq\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)
and

W=X_{0}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)
. Since

\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{0\mid t,x}[W]=\delta_{x}
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{3,0\mid t}(x)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{0\mid t,x}\left[(W-\delta_{x})\|W-\delta_{x}\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{M}_{3,0\mid t}(x)-2\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)\delta_{x}-\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\delta_{x}+2\|\delta_{x}\|_{2}^{2}\delta_{x}.

(211)

Here
\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)
is the empirical covariance centered at the
population posterior mean, defined in (
178
).
Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left\|\widehat{M}_{3,0\mid t}(X_{t})-M_{3,0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left\|\widetilde{M}_{3,0\mid t}(X_{t})-M_{3,0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{H}}_{1}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left\|\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\left(\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{H}}_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right)^{2}\left\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{H}}_{3}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{6}\right]}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{H}}_{4}}.

(212)

Bound for
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
.

We first control
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
. As in the covariance analysis above, we first
split off the non-typical region:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|\widetilde{M}_{3,0\mid t}(x)-M_{3,0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]p_{X_{t}}(x){\textnormal{d}}x+R_{0}^{6}\mathbb{P}\!\left(X_{t}\notin{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right).

(213)

The second term is controlled by Lemma
1
and is
absorbed into the final bound. It remains to bound the typical-region term.
Decompose it according to

{\mathcal{E}}_{x}=\{\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)\geq p_{X_{t}}(x)/2\}
. On
{\mathcal{E}}_{x}
, absorb

M_{3,0\mid t}(x)
into each numerator summand:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left\{\left(X_{0}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\left\|X_{0}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}-M_{3,0\mid t}(x)\right\}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\bigr)\right]=0.

(214)

Thus the denominator is bounded below by
p_{X_{t}}(x)/2
, and the cross terms
vanish by independence. Hence the good-event contribution is bounded by

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|\widetilde{M}_{3,0\mid t}(x)-M_{3,0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}}\right]p_{X_{t}}(x){\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{1}{n}\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Y,\Omega}\left[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Z\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid X_{t})}\left[\left\|Z-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{6}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}Z\bigr)}{p_{X_{t}}(X_{t})}\right]\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\right].

(215)

Applying Lemmas
25
and
26
with
\beta=6
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left\|\widetilde{M}_{3,0\mid t}(x)-M_{3,0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}}\right]p_{X_{t}}(x){\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}\left(\frac{k^{\star}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{3}+\frac{R_{0}^{6}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{6}}{n^{2}}.

(216)

On the bad event, the same deterministic argument as for the covariance gives

\left\|\widetilde{M}_{3,0\mid t}(x)-M_{3,0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}\lesssim\widehat{Q}_{3,t}(y,\omega)+m_{3,t}(y,\omega),

where

\widehat{Q}_{3,t}(y,\omega)\coloneqq\frac{\sum_{r=1}^{n}\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}^{3}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}{D(x)},\qquad m_{3,t}(y,\omega)\coloneqq\mathbb{E}_{V\sim{\mathbb{P}}_{0\mid t}(\cdot\mid x)}\left[\|V-y\|_{2}^{3}\right].

Decompose
\widehat{Q}_{3,t}
at the same type of truncation radius

\rho_{t}>0
. The local part is bounded by
\rho_{t}^{3}
, the tail part is
bounded by
R_{0}^{3}\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)
, and
m_{3,t}
is handled by the
same typical/complement posterior split as above. Therefore, under the
conditions of Lemmas
27
and
28
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left\|\widetilde{M}_{3,0\mid t}(X_{t})-M_{3,0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{X_{t}}^{c}}\mathbbm{1}\{X_{t}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\rho_{t}^{6}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}+\frac{R_{0}^{6}}{n}\left(\frac{R_{0}}{\lambda_{t}}\right)^{k^{\star}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}\left(\frac{k^{\star}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{3}+\frac{R_{0}^{6}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{6}}{n^{2}}.

(217)

We choose
\lambda_{t}=\rho_{t}/2
and

\rho_{t}\asymp R_{0}^{\frac{6}{k^{\star}+6}}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2(k^{\star}+6)}}\sqrt{\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}
.
With this choice, the second and third terms in
(
217
) are absorbed by the local empirical
term. Combining the typical-region good and bad bounds with
(
213
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left\|\widetilde{M}_{3,0\mid t}(X_{t})-M_{3,0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{36}{k^{\star}+6}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}-\frac{3k^{\star}}{k^{\star}+6}}}\log^{3}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}+\frac{R_{0}^{6}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{6}}{n^{2}}.

(218)

Bounds for
{\mathcal{H}}_{2},~{\mathcal{H}}_{3}
.

We next consider
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{3}
. Since these two terms have
essentially the same structure, we only spell out the analysis of
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
;
the term
{\mathcal{H}}_{3}
is treated analogously. In the following, write

\delta_{x}\coloneqq\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x),\qquad x=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\omega.

Let
\rho_{t}>0
be a truncation radius to be optimized later. We decompose

\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)
according to whether the empirical sample is
local around
y
, and whether the population posterior mean is local around

y
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{t}(y,\omega)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\sum\limits_{{r}={1}}^{n}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)\mathbbm{1}\{\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}\leq\rho_{t}\}}{D(x)}\left(X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)^{\otimes 2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 250.00038pt\cdot\mathbbm{1}\{\|y-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}\leq\rho_{t}\},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{t}(y,\omega)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\sum\limits_{{r}={1}}^{n}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)\mathbbm{1}\{\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\}}{D(x)}\left(X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)^{\otimes 2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 250.00038pt\cdot\mathbbm{1}\{\|y-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}\leq\rho_{t}\},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{t}(y,\omega)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\sum\limits_{{r}={1}}^{n}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}{D(x)}\left(X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)^{\otimes 2}\right)\mathbbm{1}\{\|y-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\}.

By the definition of
\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)
,

\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}_{0\mid t}(x)={\mathcal{J}}_{t}(y,\omega)+{\mathcal{K}}_{t}(y,\omega)+{\mathcal{L}}_{t}(y,\omega).

Substituting this decomposition into the definition of
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
and using

(a+b+c)^{2}\lesssim a^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left\|{\mathcal{J}}_{t}(Y,\Omega)\delta_{X_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left\|{\mathcal{K}}_{t}(Y,\Omega)\delta_{X_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left\|{\mathcal{L}}_{t}(Y,\Omega)\delta_{X_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right].

(219)

We first bound the local contribution. On the support of
{\mathcal{J}}_{t}
, both

\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}\leq\rho_{t}
and

\|y-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}\leq\rho_{t}
, so

\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}\lesssim\rho_{t}
. Hence

\|{\mathcal{J}}_{t}(y,\omega)\|\lesssim\rho_{t}^{2}
, and the same
mean-estimation argument as in the score bound gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left\|{\mathcal{J}}_{t}(Y,\Omega)\delta_{X_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{t}^{4}\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|\delta_{X_{t}}\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\rho_{t}^{4}}{n}\left(\frac{\rho_{t}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}+\frac{R_{0}^{2}}{\rho_{t}^{k^{\star}}}\right)\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left[\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\rho_{t}^{6}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}+\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+6}}{n\rho_{t}^{k^{\star}}}\right]\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}.

(220)

In the last step we used
\rho_{t}\leq R_{0}
, which is the relevant regime for
the optimized truncation radius below.

For the empirical tail contribution, define
\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)
as in
Lemma
28
. On the support of
{\mathcal{K}}_{t}
,

\|{\mathcal{K}}_{t}(y,\omega)\|_{2}\lesssim R_{0}^{2}\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)
, while

\|\delta_{x}\|_{2}\lesssim R_{0}
. Therefore Lemma
28

implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left\|{\mathcal{K}}_{t}(Y,\Omega)\delta_{X_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim R_{0}^{6}\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)^{2}\right]\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{6}N_{\rho_{t}/2}}{n}+\frac{R_{0}^{6}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{6}}{n^{2}}\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+6}}{n\rho_{t}^{k^{\star}}}.

(221)

It remains to control the event on which the population posterior center is
far from
y
. The typical-region posterior concentration estimate
(Lemma
1
and Corollary
1
)
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left\|{\mathcal{L}}_{t}(Y,\Omega)\delta_{X_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\lesssim R_{0}^{6}\,{\mathbb{P}}\!\left(\|Y-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\right)\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{6}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{6}}{n^{2}}.

(222)

Combining (
219
)–(
222
), and absorbing the

n^{-2}
term into the displayed first-order terms in the regime considered,
we obtain, for any admissible
\rho_{t}
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left[\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\rho_{t}^{6}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}+\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+6}}{n\rho_{t}^{k^{\star}}}\right]\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}.

(223)

We optimize the right-hand side by balancing the two terms:

\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\rho_{t}^{6}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}\asymp\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+6}}{\rho_{t}^{k^{\star}}},\qquad\text{equivalently}\qquad\rho_{t}^{k^{\star}+6}\asymp R_{0}^{6}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}.

Thus we choose

\rho_{t}\asymp R_{0}^{\frac{6}{k^{\star}+6}}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2(k^{\star}+6)}}.

(224)

Substituting (
224
) into
(
223
) yields the final bound of
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{36}{k^{\star}+6}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}-\frac{3k^{\star}}{k^{\star}+6}}}\log^{2}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}.

(225)

Bound for
{\mathcal{H}}_{4}
.

Finally, we control

{\mathcal{H}}_{4}=\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\!\left[\left\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{6}\right]
.
Taking
\beta=3
in Lemma
29
directly
gives

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{36}{k^{\star}+6}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}-\frac{3k^{\star}}{k^{\star}+6}}}\log^{3}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}+\frac{R_{0}^{6}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{6}}{n^{2}}.

(226)

Finally, integrating (
210
),
(
212
), (
218
),
(
223
), and (
226
) together yields the bound
required by the lemma.

I.5
Proofs of auxiliary lemmas

I.5.1
Proof of Lemma
21

Fix
2\leq\beta\leq 6
. We first isolate the contribution of a single cell in
the typical region. Fix any triple
(x_{0},y,\omega)
such that

\omega\in{\mathcal{G}},~y\in\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i},~x_{0}\notin{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)
,
and let
i=i(y)\in{\mathcal{I}}
be such that
y\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
. With

x=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\,y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\,\omega,~\gamma_{t}=\sqrt{\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}
,
we have

(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{d/2}p_{X_{t}}(x)=\int\varphi_{1}\big(\gamma_{t}(u-y)-\omega\big)p_{0}(u)\,{\textnormal{d}}u.

Thus the integrand in the lemma is

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{\beta}(x_{0},y,\omega)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\varphi_{1}^{\beta}\big(\gamma_{t}(x_{0}-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}(\omega)\frac{p_{0}(x_{0})p_{0}(y)}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\beta d/2}p_{X_{t}}^{\beta}(x)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varphi_{1}^{\beta}\big(\gamma_{t}(x_{0}-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{0}(x_{0})p_{0}(y)}{\left[\int\varphi_{1}\big(\gamma_{t}(u-y)-\omega\big)p_{0}(u)\,{\textnormal{d}}u\right]^{\beta}}.

Lower bounding the denominator by the contribution of
{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
, conditioning
on
{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
, and applying Jensen’s inequality to the convex map

z\mapsto z^{-\beta}
gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{\beta}(x_{0},y,\omega)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{\beta-1}({\mathcal{B}}_{i})}\int\frac{\varphi_{1}^{\beta}\big(\gamma_{t}(x_{0}-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}(\omega)}{\varphi_{1}^{\beta}\big(\gamma_{t}(u-y)-\omega\big)}

\displaystyle\hskip 80.00012pt\times p_{0}(x_{0})p_{0}(y\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})p_{0}(u\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})\,{\textnormal{d}}u.

(227)

Suppose
x_{0}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
, and write

\xi_{x_{0}}=x_{0}-x_{j}^{\star},~\xi_{u}=u-x_{i}^{\star}
.
For the Gaussian ratio in (
227
), the
same exponent calculation as in the
\beta=2
case gives

\displaystyle\frac{\varphi_{1}^{\beta}\big(\gamma_{t}(x_{0}-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}(\omega)}{\varphi_{1}^{\beta}\big(\gamma_{t}(u-y)-\omega\big)}

\displaystyle\qquad=(2\pi)^{-d/2}\exp\left\{-\frac{\beta}{2}\gamma_{t}^{2}\|x_{0}-y\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}\gamma_{t}^{2}\|u-y\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\|\omega\|_{2}^{2}+\beta\gamma_{t}(x_{0}-u)^{\top}\omega\right\}.

Using
\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}
, the cell diameter bound, and completing the square in

\omega
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\varphi_{1}^{\beta}\big(\gamma_{t}(x_{0}-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}(\omega)}{\varphi_{1}^{\beta}\big(\gamma_{t}(u-y)-\omega\big)}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{\beta}\exp\left\{-\frac{\beta}{2}\gamma_{t}^{2}\|x_{0}-y\|_{2}^{2}+C_{\beta}\gamma_{t}\sqrt{k^{\star}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\,\|x_{0}-y\|_{2}\right\}\varphi_{1}\big(\omega-\beta\gamma_{t}(\xi_{x_{0}}-\xi_{u})\big).

Since
x_{0}\notin{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)
, the first negative quadratic
term dominates the linear term. Enlarging constants if necessary and using

\beta\leq 6
, the preceding display is bounded by

C_{\beta}\left(\frac{R_{0}n}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{-90k^{\star}}\varphi_{1}\big(\omega-\beta\gamma_{t}(\xi_{x_{0}}-\xi_{u})\big).

Substituting this estimate into
(
227
) yields the pointwise bound

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{\beta}(x_{0},y,\omega)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\beta}}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{\beta-1}({\mathcal{B}}_{i})}\left(\frac{R_{0}n}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{-90k^{\star}}\int\varphi_{1}\big(\omega-\beta\gamma_{t}(\xi_{x_{0}}-\xi_{u})\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 80.00012pt\times p_{0}(x_{0})p_{0}(y\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})p_{0}(u\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})\,{\textnormal{d}}u.

(228)

We now sum over the cells containing
y
and
x_{0}
. For fixed

(x_{0},y,u)
, the shifted Gaussian density integrates to at most one over

\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{y\in\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\int_{\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}}\int_{x_{0}\notin{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)}\mathsf{T}_{\beta}(x_{0},y,\omega){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{\beta}\left(\frac{R_{0}n}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{-90k^{\star}}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{\epsilon_{0}}}\frac{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{j})}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{\beta-1}({\mathcal{B}}_{i})}\leq C_{\beta}\left(\frac{R_{0}n}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{-90k^{\star}}N_{\epsilon_{0}}^{\beta+1}\ll\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}{n}.

The last step uses the covering bound and the definition of
{\mathcal{I}}
; the
large negative exponent was chosen with enough slack to absorb the fixed

\beta\leq 6
. This proves the lemma.

I.5.2
Proof of Lemma
22

The argument is parallel to the proof of
Lemma
21
; the only difference is that the
denominator now contains the third power of
p_{X_{t}}(x)
. Fix

(x_{0},v,y,\omega)
such that

\omega\in{\mathcal{G}},~y\in\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i},~v\notin{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)
,
and let
i=i(y)\in{\mathcal{I}}
be such that
y\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
. As before, write

x=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\,y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\,\omega,~\gamma_{t}=\sqrt{\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}
.
Then

(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{d/2}p_{X_{t}}(x)=\int\varphi_{1}\big(\gamma_{t}(u-y)-\omega\big)p_{0}(u)\,{\textnormal{d}}u.

We lower bound this denominator by retaining only the mass from the cell

{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
. Since the denominator is cubed, conditioning on
{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}(x_{0},v,y,\omega)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{2}({\mathcal{B}}_{i})}\frac{\varphi_{1}^{2}\big(\gamma_{t}(x_{0}-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}\big(\gamma_{t}(v-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{0}(x_{0})p_{0}(y\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})p_{0}(v)}{\left[\int\varphi_{1}\big(\gamma_{t}(u-y)-\omega\big)p_{0}(u\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})\,{\textnormal{d}}u\right]^{3}}.

(229)

Here the factor
1/{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{2}({\mathcal{B}}_{i})
comes from one factor of

{\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{i})
in
p_{0}(y)={\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{i})p_{0}(y\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})
and three factors of

{\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{i})
in the restricted denominator.

Next, since
x\mapsto x^{-3}
is convex on
(0,\infty)
, Jensen’s
inequality yields

\left[\int\varphi_{1}\big(\gamma_{t}(u-y)-\omega\big)p_{0}(u\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})\,{\textnormal{d}}u\right]^{-3}\leq\int\varphi_{1}^{-3}\big(\gamma_{t}(u-y)-\omega\big)p_{0}(u\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})\,{\textnormal{d}}u.

Combining this estimate with
(
229
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}(x_{0},v,y,\omega)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{2}({\mathcal{B}}_{i})}\int\frac{\varphi_{1}^{2}\big(\gamma_{t}(x_{0}-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}\big(\gamma_{t}(v-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}(\omega)}{\varphi_{1}^{3}\big(\gamma_{t}(u-y)-\omega\big)}

\displaystyle\hskip 80.00012pt\times p_{0}(x_{0})p_{0}(v)p_{0}(y\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})p_{0}(u\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})\,{\textnormal{d}}u.

(230)

We now bound the Gaussian ratio in the integrand. Suppose
v\in{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
and

x_{0}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}
, and write

\xi_{x_{0}}\coloneqq x_{0}-x_{\ell}^{\star},\qquad\xi_{v}\coloneqq v-x_{j}^{\star},\qquad\xi_{u}\coloneqq u-x_{i}^{\star},\qquad\xi_{y}\coloneqq y-x_{i}^{\star}.

We spell out the Gaussian calculation directly. Since

\varphi_{1}(z)=(2\pi)^{-d/2}\exp(-\left\|{z}\right\|_{2}^{2}/2)
, the ratio equals

\displaystyle\frac{\varphi_{1}^{2}\big(\gamma_{t}(x_{0}-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}\big(\gamma_{t}(v-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}(\omega)}{\varphi_{1}^{3}\big(\gamma_{t}(u-y)-\omega\big)}

\displaystyle\quad=(2\pi)^{-d/2}\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\omega}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\gamma_{t}\big(2(x_{0}-y)+(v-y)-3(u-y)\big)^{\top}\omega\right.
