\displaystyle\hskip 120.00018pt\left.-\gamma_{t}^{2}\left\|{x_{0}-y}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\left\|{v-y}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{3\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\left\|{u-y}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}.

(231)

The only place where the denominator cell point enters is through

u-y=\xi_{u}-\xi_{y}
.
Similarly,

x_{0}-y=(\xi_{x_{0}}-\xi_{y})+(x_{\ell}^{\star}-x_{i}^{\star}),\qquad v-y=(\xi_{v}-\xi_{y})+(x_{j}^{\star}-x_{i}^{\star}).

Thus the terms generated by
u-y
,
\xi_{x_{0}}-\xi_{y}
, and

\xi_{v}-\xi_{y}
are local cell errors. We separate the part of the linear term
that points in directions between cell representatives:

2(x_{0}-y)+(v-y)-3(u-y)=\bigl(2\xi_{x_{0}}+\xi_{v}-3\xi_{u}\bigr)+2(x_{\ell}^{\star}-x_{i}^{\star})+(x_{j}^{\star}-x_{i}^{\star}).

Since
\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}
, the definition of
{\mathcal{G}}
gives

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}\bigl\{2(x_{\ell}^{\star}-x_{i}^{\star})+(x_{j}^{\star}-x_{i}^{\star})\bigr\}^{\top}\omega

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 6\gamma_{t}\sqrt{k^{\star}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\,\left\|{x_{\ell}^{\star}-x_{i}^{\star}}\right\|_{2}+3\gamma_{t}\sqrt{k^{\star}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\,\left\|{x_{j}^{\star}-x_{i}^{\star}}\right\|_{2}.

(232)

For the local part, we complete the square in
\omega
. In particular, for
fixed
x_{0},v,u,y
,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\omega}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\gamma_{t}\bigl(2\xi_{x_{0}}+\xi_{v}-3\xi_{u}\bigr)^{\top}\omega

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\omega-\gamma_{t}(2\xi_{x_{0}}+\xi_{v}-3\xi_{u})}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\left\|{2\xi_{x_{0}}+\xi_{v}-3\xi_{u}}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

(233)

The additional square in (
233
) is harmless:
since
x_{0}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}
,
v\in{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
, and
u,y\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
,

\gamma_{t}^{2}\left\|{\xi_{x_{0}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1,\qquad\gamma_{t}^{2}\left\|{\xi_{v}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1,\qquad\gamma_{t}^{2}\left\|{\xi_{u}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1,\qquad\gamma_{t}^{2}\left\|{\xi_{y}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1,

and hence

\gamma_{t}^{2}\left\|{2\xi_{x_{0}}+\xi_{v}-3\xi_{u}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\gamma_{t}^{2}\left\|{u-y}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 40

Combining this estimate with
(
231
) and
(
232
), the exponent is bounded by

\displaystyle-\gamma_{t}^{2}\left\|{x_{0}-y}\right\|_{2}^{2}+6\gamma_{t}\sqrt{k^{\star}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\,\left\|{x_{\ell}^{\star}-x_{i}^{\star}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\left\|{v-y}\right\|_{2}^{2}+3\gamma_{t}\sqrt{k^{\star}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\,\left\|{x_{j}^{\star}-x_{i}^{\star}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\omega-\gamma_{t}(2\xi_{x_{0}}+\xi_{v}-3\xi_{u})}\right\|_{2}^{2}+40.

(234)

Next,

\left\|{x_{\ell}^{\star}-x_{i}^{\star}}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|{x_{0}-y}\right\|_{2}+2\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}},\qquad\left\|{x_{j}^{\star}-x_{i}^{\star}}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|{v-y}\right\|_{2}+2\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}.

Absorbing the preceding
40
into the exponential prefactor, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\varphi_{1}^{2}\big(\gamma_{t}(x_{0}-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}\big(\gamma_{t}(v-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}(\omega)}{\varphi_{1}^{3}\big(\gamma_{t}(u-y)-\omega\big)}

\displaystyle\quad\leq e^{40}\exp\left\{-\gamma_{t}^{2}\left\|{x_{0}-y}\right\|_{2}^{2}+6\gamma_{t}\sqrt{k^{\star}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\,\left\|{x_{0}-y}\right\|_{2}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt\left.-\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\left\|{v-y}\right\|_{2}^{2}+3\gamma_{t}\sqrt{k^{\star}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\,\left\|{v-y}\right\|_{2}\right\}\varphi_{1}\big(\omega-\gamma_{t}(2\xi_{x_{0}}+\xi_{v}-3\xi_{u})\big).

(235)

The
x_{0}
-dependent exponent is a concave quadratic and is uniformly bounded
above by its maximum:

-\gamma_{t}^{2}\left\|{x_{0}-y}\right\|_{2}^{2}+6\gamma_{t}\sqrt{k^{\star}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\,\left\|{x_{0}-y}\right\|_{2}\leq 9k^{\star}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}.

On the other hand,
v\notin{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)
gives

\left\|{v-y}\right\|_{2}\geq\frac{15}{\gamma_{t}}\sqrt{k^{\star}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}.

Thus the exponent in (
235
) is bounded
above by

9k^{\star}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}-\frac{15^{2}}{2}k^{\star}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}+3\cdot 15\,k^{\star}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}=-\frac{117}{2}k^{\star}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\leq-50k^{\star}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\frac{\varphi_{1}^{2}\big(\gamma_{t}(x_{0}-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}\big(\gamma_{t}(v-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}(\omega)}{\varphi_{1}^{3}\big(\gamma_{t}(u-y)-\omega\big)}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq e^{40}\left(\frac{R_{0}n}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{-50k^{\star}}\varphi_{1}\big(\omega-\gamma_{t}(2\xi_{x_{0}}+\xi_{v}-3\xi_{u})\big).

(236)

Plugging (
236
) into
(
230
) yields the pointwise bound

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}(x_{0},v,y,\omega)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{40}}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{2}({\mathcal{B}}_{i})}\left(\frac{R_{0}n}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{-50k^{\star}}\int\varphi_{1}\big(\omega-\gamma_{t}(2\xi_{x_{0}}+\xi_{v}-3\xi_{u})\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 80.00012pt\times p_{0}(x_{0})p_{0}(v)p_{0}(y\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})p_{0}(u\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})\,{\textnormal{d}}u.

(237)

Finally, decompose the integral in Lemma
22

according to the cells containing
y
,
v
, and
x_{0}
. For every fixed

(i,j,\ell)
, applying (
237
) gives

\displaystyle\int_{y\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\int_{\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}}\int_{x_{0}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}}\int_{v\in{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\backslash{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)}\mathsf{S}(x_{0},v,y,\omega)\,{\textnormal{d}}v\,{\textnormal{d}}x_{0}\,{\textnormal{d}}\omega\,{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{e^{40}}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{2}({\mathcal{B}}_{i})}\left(\frac{R_{0}n}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{-50k^{\star}}\int_{y\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\int_{x_{0}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}}\int_{v\in{\mathcal{B}}_{j}}\int_{u\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\int_{\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}}\varphi_{1}\big(\omega-\gamma_{t}(2\xi_{x_{0}}+\xi_{v}-3\xi_{u})\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 120.00018pt\times p_{0}(x_{0})p_{0}(v)p_{0}(y\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})p_{0}(u\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})\,{\textnormal{d}}\omega\,{\textnormal{d}}u\,{\textnormal{d}}v\,{\textnormal{d}}x_{0}\,{\textnormal{d}}y.

(238)

For fixed
x_{0},v,y,u
, the last Gaussian term is the density of

\mathcal{N}(\gamma_{t}(2\xi_{x_{0}}+\xi_{v}-3\xi_{u}),I_{d})
as a function of
\omega
. Hence,
enlarging
{\mathcal{G}}
to
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and integrating the conditional densities in

y
and
u
gives

\displaystyle\int_{y\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\int_{x_{0}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}}\int_{v\in{\mathcal{B}}_{j}}\int_{u\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\int_{\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}}\varphi_{1}\big(\omega-\gamma_{t}(2\xi_{x_{0}}+\xi_{v}-3\xi_{u})\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\times p_{0}(x_{0})p_{0}(v)p_{0}(y\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})p_{0}(u\mid{\mathcal{B}}_{i})\,{\textnormal{d}}\omega\,{\textnormal{d}}u\,{\textnormal{d}}v\,{\textnormal{d}}x_{0}\,{\textnormal{d}}y\leq{\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}){\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{j}).

(239)

We now sum the cellwise bounds. First decompose the full integral according to
the cells containing
y
,
v
, and
x_{0}
:

\displaystyle\int_{y\in\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\int_{\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}}\int_{x_{0}}\int_{v\notin{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)}\mathsf{S}(x_{0},v,y,\omega)\,{\textnormal{d}}v\,{\textnormal{d}}x_{0}\,{\textnormal{d}}\omega\,{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{\epsilon_{0}}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N_{\epsilon_{0}}}\int_{y\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\int_{\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}}\int_{x_{0}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}}\int_{v\in{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\backslash{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)}\mathsf{S}(x_{0},v,y,\omega)\,{\textnormal{d}}v\,{\textnormal{d}}x_{0}\,{\textnormal{d}}\omega\,{\textnormal{d}}y.

(240)

Combining (
238
) and
(
239
), each
(i,j,\ell)
-summand is
bounded by

\frac{e^{40}}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{2}({\mathcal{B}}_{i})}\left(\frac{R_{0}n}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{-50k^{\star}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}){\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{j}).

Substituting this into (
240
) gives

\displaystyle\int_{y\in\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\int_{\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}}\int_{x_{0}}\int_{v\notin{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)}\mathsf{S}(x_{0},v,y,\omega)\,{\textnormal{d}}v\,{\textnormal{d}}x_{0}\,{\textnormal{d}}\omega\,{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\qquad\leq e^{40}\left(\frac{R_{0}n}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{-50k^{\star}}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{\epsilon_{0}}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N_{\epsilon_{0}}}\frac{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{j}){\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{\ell})}{{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{2}({\mathcal{B}}_{i})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq e^{40}\left(\frac{R_{0}n}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{-50k^{\star}}|{\mathcal{I}}|\,\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{N_{\epsilon_{0}}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{j})\sum_{\ell=1}^{N_{\epsilon_{0}}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{\ell})}{\min_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{2}({\mathcal{B}}_{i})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq e^{40}\left(\frac{R_{0}n}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{-50k^{\star}}N_{\epsilon_{0}}^{5}\leq e^{40}\left(\frac{R_{0}n}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{-50k^{\star}}\left(\frac{R_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\right)^{5k^{\star}}\ll\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}{n}.

Here we used
\sum_{j}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{j})\leq 1
,
\sum_{\ell}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{\ell})\leq 1
,
and the definition of
{\mathcal{I}}
to bound

|{\mathcal{I}}|/\min_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{2}({\mathcal{B}}_{i})
by the displayed covering factor. This
proves the Lemma.

I.5.3
Proof of Lemma
23

If
d>(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}
, then

\widetilde{\varphi}_{t,x}(z)=\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}z-x)
, so
m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)=m_{t}(x)
.
The pointwise integrand bound is also trivial in this case. Hence we only
consider the regime
d\leq(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}
.

We first prove the pointwise integrand bound. Note that for any positive
number
\lambda
,
\lambda e^{-\lambda/6}\leq 3
. Denote

\Lambda=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}}\!\left[\left\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x\right\|_{2}^{2}\left(\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\bigr)-\widetilde{\varphi}_{t,x}(X_{0})\right)^{2}\right]}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|\Lambda\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}(\Lambda)\mathbbm{1}\{\|\Lambda\|_{2}>a_{t}\}\right]}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\leq\frac{1}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{d/2}}\left(\frac{\|\Lambda\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}e^{-\|\Lambda\|_{2}^{2}/(6(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}))}\right)e^{-a_{t}^{2}/(3(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}))}\frac{\mathbb{E}\!\left[\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x\bigr)\right]}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\leq\frac{3e^{-a_{t}^{2}/(3(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}))}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{d/2}}\lesssim\left(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{R_{0}n}\right)^{2d}.

(241)

where the penultimate inequality follows from

\frac{\mathbb{E}\!\left[\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x\bigr)\right]}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}=\int\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x\bigr)p_{X_{0}}(X_{0})}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}{\textnormal{d}}X_{0}=1.

The last inequality holds since

a_{t}\geq 3\sqrt{d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}
.

We next prove the typical-region bound. Suppose

x\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}
.
Since
x\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}
, by
(
41
) there exist

x_{0}^{\star}\in\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
and
\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}
such that

x=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}^{\star}+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\,\omega.

Let
i(x)\in{\mathcal{I}}
be such that
x_{0}^{\star}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i(x)}
. Then Bayes’
formula and the definition of
m_{t}^{\rm th}
give

\displaystyle\|m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)-m_{t}(x)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\int\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}z-x\|_{2}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}z)\mathbbm{1}\{\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}z-x\|_{2}>a_{t}\}p_{0}(z){\textnormal{d}}z}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}.

(242)

Here we used

|\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}z)-\widetilde{\varphi}_{t,x}(z)|\leq\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}z)\mathbbm{1}\{\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}z-x\|_{2}>a_{t}\}
.

We lower bound the denominator using only the cell
{\mathcal{B}}_{i(x)}
:

\displaystyle p_{X_{t}}(x)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{{\mathcal{B}}_{i(x)}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u)p_{0}(u){\textnormal{d}}u

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{P}}_{X_{0}}({\mathcal{B}}_{i(x)})\inf_{u\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i(x)}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u).

(243)

Since
x\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}
, the definition of
{\mathcal{I}}
gives

{\mathbb{P}}_{X_{0}}({\mathcal{B}}_{i(x)})\geq\exp\{-C_{1}k\log T\}
. Moreover, for every

u\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i(x)}
,

\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\|\omega\|_{2}+\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\|x_{0}^{\star}-u\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(2\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{C_{1}k\log T}\bigr)+2\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\epsilon_{0},

where the last step follows from
\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}
and

x_{0}^{\star},u\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i(x)}
. Thus

\displaystyle p_{X_{t}}(x)

\displaystyle\geq c(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{-d/2}\exp\{-Cd-Ck\log T\}\exp\{-C_{1}k\log T\}.

(244)

It remains to bound the numerator in
(
242
). For every
z
,

\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}z-x\|_{2}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}z)\mathbbm{1}\{\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}z-x\|_{2}>a_{t}\}\leq C(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{-d/2}a_{t}\exp\left\{-\frac{a_{t}^{2}}{4(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right\}.

Since

a_{t}^{2}=C_{\mathsf{th}}^{2}d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}},

choosing
C_{\mathsf{th}}
sufficiently large gives

a_{t}\exp\left\{-\frac{a_{t}^{2}}{4(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right\}\leq\exp\{-Cd-Ck\log T-C_{1}k\log T\}\left(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{R_{0}n}\right)^{2d}.

Consequently, the numerator in
(
242
) is at most

C(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{-d/2}\exp\{-Cd-Ck\log T-C_{1}k\log T\}\left(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{R_{0}n}\right)^{2d}.

Combining this with (
244
) yields

\|m_{t}^{\rm th}(x)-m_{t}(x)\|_{2}\leq C\left(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{R_{0}n}\right)^{2d}.

Absorbing the universal constant into the sufficiently large threshold
constant completes the proof.

I.5.4
Proof of Lemma
24

Recall that
\gamma_{t}=\sqrt{\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}
.
Let
\{B_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N_{\gamma_{t}^{-1}}({\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}})}

be a measurable partition induced by a

\gamma_{t}^{-1}\left(=\sqrt{\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\right)

-cover of
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}}
, and write

m_{j}={\mathbb{P}}_{X_{0}}(B_{j})
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\bigr)p_{0}(x_{0}){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}{\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{\sum_{j}m_{j}\int_{B_{j}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\bigr)p_{0}(x_{0}\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}}{\sum_{i}m_{i}\int_{B_{i}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u\bigr)p_{0}(u\mid B_{i}){\textnormal{d}}u}{\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{\leq}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{\gamma_{t}^{-1}}({\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}})}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{\int_{B_{j}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\bigr)p_{0}(x_{0}\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}}{\int_{B_{j}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u\bigr)p_{0}(u\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}u}{\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(b)}}{\leq}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{\gamma_{t}^{-1}}({\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}})}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{B_{j}}\int_{B_{j}}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\bigr)}{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u\bigr)}p_{0}(x_{0}\mid B_{j})p_{0}(u\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}u{\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(c)}}{=}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{\gamma_{t}^{-1}}({\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}})}\int_{B_{j}}\int_{B_{j}}\exp\left\{\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}\|x_{0}-u\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right\}p_{0}(x_{0}\mid B_{j})p_{0}(u\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}u{\textnormal{d}}x_{0}.

(245)

Here, (a) follows by lower bounding the denominator in the
j
-th numerator term
using only the contribution from the same cell
B_{j}
. (b) is Jensen’s
inequality applied to
z\mapsto z^{-1}
under the conditional law

p_{0}(\cdot\mid B_{j})
. For (c), completing the square gives

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\bigr)}{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u\bigr)}{\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}\|x_{0}-u\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right\}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(2x_{0}-u)\bigr){\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}\|x_{0}-u\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right\}.

Since each
B_{j}
has diameter at most
\sqrt{\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}
, the exponential
factor is at most
C
. The covering assumption then yields

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\bigr)p_{0}(x_{0}){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}{\textnormal{d}}x\leq CN_{\gamma_{t}^{-1}}({\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}})\leq C\left(\frac{R_{0}\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\right)^{k^{\star}}.

This proves the lemma.

I.5.5
Proof of Lemma
25

Fix an integer
\beta\geq 2
.
Recall that

\gamma_{t}=\sqrt{\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}
.
Expanding the posterior expectation in the definition of
{\mathcal{J}}_{1,\beta}

gives

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{1,\beta}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}}\times{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\|x_{0}-y\|_{2}^{\beta}\frac{\varphi_{1}^{2}\bigl(\gamma_{t}(x_{0}-y)-\omega\bigr)\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{0}(x_{0})p_{0}(y)}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{d}p_{X_{t}}^{2}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\omega\bigr)}{\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}y{\textnormal{d}}\omega.

(246)

Define

\mathsf{T}(x_{0},y,\omega)\coloneqq\varphi_{1}^{2}\bigl(\gamma_{t}(x_{0}-y)-\omega\bigr)\varphi_{1}(\omega)\frac{p_{0}(x_{0})p_{0}(y)}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{d}p_{X_{t}}^{2}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\omega\bigr)}.

Recall that

{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(y)=\left\{v\in{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}}:\sqrt{\alpha}\|v-y\|_{2}\leq 15\sqrt{k^{\star}(1-\alpha)\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\alpha}}\right\}.

Thus, with
\alpha=\overline{\alpha}_{t}
,

\|x_{0}-y\|_{2}^{\beta}\leq\left(\frac{225k^{\star}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{\beta/2}+R_{0}^{\beta}\mathbbm{1}\{x_{0}\notin{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)\}.

Therefore, integrating this with
y,\omega\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}=\left(\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\right)\times{\mathcal{G}}
yields,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{1,\beta}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta}\left(\frac{k^{\star}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{\beta/2}\int_{y\in\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\int_{\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}}\int_{x_{0}}\mathsf{T}(x_{0},y,\omega){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\quad+R_{0}^{\beta}\int_{y\in\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\int_{\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}}\int_{x_{0}\notin{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)}\mathsf{T}(x_{0},y,\omega){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y.

(247)

The first integral in (
247
) can be reduced to
Lemma
24
. Indeed, enlarging the domains to

{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and changing variables

x=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\omega
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{y\in\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\int_{\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}}\int_{x_{0}}\mathsf{T}(x_{0},y,\omega){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{x,y,x_{0}}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\bigr)\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y\bigr)p_{0}(x_{0})p_{0}(y)}{p_{X_{t}}^{2}(x)}{\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}y{\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{x,x_{0}}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\bigr)p_{0}(x_{0})}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}{\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}x\leq C\left(\frac{R_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\right)^{k^{\star}}.

(248)

Here the equality uses the definition of
p_{X_{t}}(x)
, and the last
inequality is Lemma
24
. The second integral in
(
247
) is controlled by the same cellwise tail estimate as
in Lemma
21
; since
\beta
is fixed, the
final super-polynomial factor there can be made smaller than

(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\beta}/n
at the cost of changing only the

\beta
-dependent constant. Hence

{\mathcal{J}}_{1,\beta}\lesssim_{\beta}\frac{(k^{\star})^{\beta/2}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\left(\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{\beta/2}}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{\beta/2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2-\beta/2}}\;+\;\frac{R_{0}^{\beta}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\beta}}{n}.

This proves the lemma.

I.5.6
Proof of Lemma
26

Fix an integer
\beta\geq 2
.
Set

\gamma_{t}=\sqrt{\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}
.
Define
\mathsf{T}
as in the proof of Lemma
25
. Also define

\mathsf{S}(x_{0},v,y,\omega)\coloneqq\frac{\varphi_{1}^{2}\big(\gamma_{t}(x_{0}-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}\big(\gamma_{t}(v-y)-\omega\big)\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{0}(x_{0})p_{0}(y)p_{0}(v)}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{3d/2}p_{X_{t}}^{3}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\omega\bigr)}.

Expanding the posterior expectation in the definition of
{\mathcal{J}}_{2,\beta}

gives

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{2,\beta}

\displaystyle=\int_{x_{0},v,y,\omega}\|y-v\|_{2}^{\beta}\mathsf{S}(x_{0},v,y,\omega){\textnormal{d}}v{\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}y{\textnormal{d}}\omega.

(249)

For
y\in\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
,
\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}
, and

x=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\omega
,
we split the
v
-integral according to whether

v\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)
. On the typical part,

\|y-v\|_{2}^{\beta}\lesssim_{\beta}\left(\frac{k^{\star}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{\beta/2},

while on the complement we use
\|y-v\|_{2}^{\beta}\leq R_{0}^{\beta}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{2,\beta}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta}\left(\frac{k^{\star}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{\beta/2}\int_{y\in\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\int_{\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}}\int_{x_{0}}\mathsf{T}(x_{0},y,\omega){\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\quad+R_{0}^{\beta}\int_{y\in\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\int_{\omega\in{\mathcal{G}}}\int_{x_{0}}\int_{v\notin{\mathcal{E}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(y)}\mathsf{S}(x_{0},v,y,\omega){\textnormal{d}}v{\textnormal{d}}x_{0}{\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y.

(250)

The first integral is bounded by (
248
), while the
last integral is controlled by the same cellwise tail estimate as in
Lemma
22
; again the final
super-polynomial factor is smaller than
(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\beta}/n

after changing only the
\beta
-dependent constant. Therefore,

{\mathcal{J}}_{2,\beta}\lesssim_{\beta}\frac{(k^{\star})^{\beta/2}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\left(\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{\beta/2}}{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{\beta/2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2-\beta/2}}\;+\;\frac{R_{0}^{\beta}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\beta}}{n}.

This proves the lemma.

I.5.7
Proof of Lemma
27

Under the change of variables

x=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\omega
, the
measure
\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y
is mapped to

p_{X_{t}}(x){\textnormal{d}}x
. Hence

\displaystyle\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{P}\!\left(\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)<\frac{1}{2}p_{X_{t}}(x)\right)\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\qquad=\int\mathbb{P}\!\left(\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)<\frac{1}{2}p_{X_{t}}(x)\right)p_{X_{t}}(x){\textnormal{d}}x\leq 4\int\frac{\mathbb{E}\bigl[(\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)-p_{X_{t}}(x))^{2}\bigr]}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}{\textnormal{d}}x.

(251)

The last inequality follows from

\left\{\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)<\frac{1}{2}p_{X_{t}}(x)\right\}\subseteq\left\{|\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)-p_{X_{t}}(x)|\geq\frac{1}{2}p_{X_{t}}(x)\right\}

and Markov’s inequality.

Let
\{B_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N}
be a cover of
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}}
by Euclidean balls of
radius
\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}
, where, by Assumption
2

and the convention
k^{\star}=C_{\mathsf{cover}}k
,

N_{\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\big({\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}}\big)\leq\left(\frac{R_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\right)^{k^{\star}}.

Without loss of generality, we take the sets
B_{j}
to be disjoint after replacing them by a
measurable partition subordinate to the cover. Write
m_{j}={\mathbb{P}}_{X_{0}}(B_{j})
and ignore indices with

m_{j}=0
. Since

p_{X_{t}}(x)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y\bigr)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}y,\qquad\hat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{i}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr),

we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[(\hat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)-p_{X_{t}}(x))^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{Var}\bigl(\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{1}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y\bigr)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}y.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{\mathbb{E}\bigl[(\hat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)-p_{X_{t}}(x))^{2}\bigr]}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}\,{\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{\int\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y\bigr)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}y}{\int\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y\bigr)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}y}\,{\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{N_{\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}({\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}})}m_{j}\int_{B_{j}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y\bigr)\,p_{X_{0}}(y\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}y}{\sum_{j=1}^{N_{\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}({\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}})}m_{j}\int_{B_{j}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y\bigr)\,p_{X_{0}}(y\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}y}\,{\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}({\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}})}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{m_{j}\int_{B_{j}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y\bigr)p_{X_{0}}(y\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}y}{\sum_{i=1}^{N_{\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}({\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}})}m_{i}\int_{B_{i}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u\bigr)p_{X_{0}}(u\mid B_{i}){\textnormal{d}}u}{\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}({\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}})}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{\int_{B_{j}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y\bigr)\,p_{X_{0}}(y\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}y}{\int_{B_{j}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u\bigr)\,p_{X_{0}}(u\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}u}\,{\textnormal{d}}x.

Here, the last inequality holds by using

\sum_{i=1}^{N_{\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}({\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}})}m_{i}\int_{B_{i}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u\bigr)p_{X_{0}}(u\mid B_{i}){\textnormal{d}}u\geq m_{j}\int_{B_{j}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u\bigr)p_{X_{0}}(u\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}u

for the denominator of every term in the summation.
It remains to control each term in the last sum. For a fixed
j
, by Jensen’s inequality
applied to the convex map
z\mapsto 1/z
,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{\int_{B_{j}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y\bigr)\,p_{X_{0}}(y\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}y}{\int_{B_{j}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u\bigr)\,p_{X_{0}}(u\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}u}\,{\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{B_{j}}\int_{B_{j}}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y\bigr)}{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u\bigr)}\,p_{X_{0}}(u\mid B_{j})p_{X_{0}}(y\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}u{\textnormal{d}}y{\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{j}}\int_{B_{j}}\left\{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y\bigr)}{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u\bigr)}\,{\textnormal{d}}x\right\}p_{X_{0}}(u\mid B_{j})p_{X_{0}}(y\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}u{\textnormal{d}}y.

The inner Gaussian integral can be computed explicitly. Indeed,

\displaystyle\int\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y\bigr)}{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u\bigr)}\,{\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle=\int(2\pi(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}))^{-d/2}\exp\left\{-\frac{\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}+\frac{\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right\}dx

\displaystyle=\int(2\pi(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}))^{-d/2}\exp\left\{-\frac{\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}(2y-u)\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}+\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}\|y-u\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right\}dx

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}\|y-u\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right\}.

Since
y,u\in B_{j}
and each
B_{j}
has radius
\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}
, we have

\|y-u\|_{2}\leq 2\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}
, and hence

\displaystyle\int\frac{\int_{B_{j}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y\bigr)\,p_{X_{0}}(y\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}y}{\int_{B_{j}}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}u\bigr)\,p_{X_{0}}(u\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}u}\,{\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{j}}\int_{B_{j}}\exp\left\{\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}\|y-u\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right\}p_{X_{0}}(u\mid B_{j})p_{X_{0}}(y\mid B_{j}){\textnormal{d}}u{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\leq e^{4}.

Combining the above estimates gives

\int\frac{\mathbb{E}\bigl[(\hat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)-p_{X_{t}}(x))^{2}\bigr]}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}\,{\textnormal{d}}x\leq\frac{e^{4}N_{\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}({\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}})}{n}\leq\frac{e^{4}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}}{n\,(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}},

which, together with (
251
), proves the
lemma.

I.5.8
Proof of Lemma
28

For
y\in{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}}
, let
i(y)
be such that

y\in{\mathcal{C}}_{i(y)}
, and define

{\mathcal{H}}_{y}\coloneqq\left\{\exists r\in[n]\colon X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{i(y)}\right\}.

Also define

{\mathcal{R}}_{\omega}\coloneqq\left\{\exists r,s\in[n]\colon\bigl\langle X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-X_{s}^{\mathsf{sample}},\omega\bigr\rangle>4\sqrt{\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\,\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-X_{s}^{\mathsf{sample}}\|_{2}\right\}.

We first split the integral according to
{\mathcal{H}}_{y}^{c}
,

{\mathcal{H}}_{y}\cap{\mathcal{R}}_{\omega}
, and
{\mathcal{H}}_{y}\cap{\mathcal{R}}_{\omega}^{c}
:

\displaystyle\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)^{2}\right]\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y=\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{y}^{c}}\right]\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt+\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{y}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\omega}}\right]\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\hskip 110.00017pt+\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{y}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\omega}^{c}}\right]\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{H}}_{y}^{c})p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}y+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathbb{P}_{\omega}({\mathcal{R}}_{\omega}\mid X_{1}^{\mathsf{sample}},\ldots,X_{n}^{\mathsf{sample}})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt+\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{y}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\omega}^{c}}\right]\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y.

(252)

In the last inequality, for the first term we dropped

\mathsf{R}_{t}^{2}
and the Gaussian integral; for the second term we dropped

\mathsf{R}_{t}^{2}
and
\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{y}}
, and then conditioned on the
training data. These steps use
0\leq\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)\leq 1
, which follows because

\mathsf{R}_{t}
is a sub-ratio of nonnegative kernel sums with denominator

D(x)
.

We first bound the first term in (
252
). For

y\in{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
, the event
{\mathcal{H}}_{y}^{c}
is exactly the event that none of the

n
training samples falls in
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
. Hence

\displaystyle\int\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{H}}_{y}^{c})p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N_{\lambda_{t}}}\int_{y\in{\mathcal{C}}_{i}}\bigl(1-{\mathbb{P}}_{X_{0}}({\mathcal{C}}_{i})\bigr)^{n}p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N_{\lambda_{t}}}{\mathbb{P}}_{X_{0}}({\mathcal{C}}_{i})\bigl(1-{\mathbb{P}}_{X_{0}}({\mathcal{C}}_{i})\bigr)^{n}\leq\frac{N_{\lambda_{t}}}{en}.

(253)

The last inequality uses
u(1-u)^{n}\leq(en)^{-1}
for
u\in[0,1]
.

Next, conditional on the training data,
\omega\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,I_{d})
is independent
of
X_{1}^{\mathsf{sample}},\ldots,X_{n}^{\mathsf{sample}}
. A Gaussian tail bound
and a union bound over
n^{2}
pairs yield

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\omega}({\mathcal{R}}_{\omega}\mid X_{1}^{\mathsf{sample}},\ldots,X_{n}^{\mathsf{sample}})

\displaystyle\leq n^{2}\exp\left\{-8\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right\}

\displaystyle=n^{2}\left(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{R_{0}n}\right)^{8}\leq\frac{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{6}}{n^{2}}.

(254)

The last step uses the parameter regime
R_{0}n/(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\geq 1
.

It remains to control the last term in (
252
).
On
{\mathcal{H}}_{y}
, choose
r_{y}
such that

X_{r_{y}}^{\mathsf{sample}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{i(y)}
, and therefore

\|X_{r_{y}}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}\leq\lambda_{t}
. Consider a summand under the
indicator
\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}>\rho_{t}
. The Gaussian ratio equals

\displaystyle\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r_{y}}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\quad=\exp\left\{-\frac{\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\|_{2}^{2}-\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r_{y}}^{\mathsf{sample}}\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad=\exp\left\{\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\left(\|X_{r_{y}}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}^{2}-\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}^{2}\right)+\gamma_{t}\bigl\langle X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-X_{r_{y}}^{\mathsf{sample}},\omega\bigr\rangle\right\}.

(255)

On
{\mathcal{R}}_{\omega}^{c}
,

\bigl\langle X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-X_{r_{y}}^{\mathsf{sample}},\omega\bigr\rangle\leq 4\sqrt{\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\,\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-X_{r_{y}}^{\mathsf{sample}}\|_{2}.

Moreover,

\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-X_{r_{y}}^{\mathsf{sample}}\|_{2}\leq\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}+\lambda_{t}.

Substituting these estimates into (
255
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r_{y}}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\exp\left\{\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\left(\lambda_{t}^{2}-\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}^{2}\right)+4\gamma_{t}\sqrt{\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\,\left(\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}+\lambda_{t}\right)\right\}.

(256)

The exponent in (
256
), as a function of

\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}
, is decreasing on the region

\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}>\rho_{t}
because

\rho_{t}\geq 32\gamma_{t}^{-1}\sqrt{\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}.

Using
\lambda_{t}\leq\rho_{t}/2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r_{y}}^{\mathsf{sample}}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\left(\lambda_{t}^{2}-\rho_{t}^{2}\right)+4\gamma_{t}\sqrt{\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\,\left(\rho_{t}+\lambda_{t}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\exp\left\{\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{\rho_{t}^{2}}{4}-\rho_{t}^{2}\right)+6\gamma_{t}\rho_{t}\sqrt{\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{3\gamma_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}^{2}}{8}+\frac{3\gamma_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}^{2}}{16}\right\}\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}^{2}}{8}\right\}.

(257)

On
{\mathcal{H}}_{y}
, the denominator
D(x)
is lower bounded by the

r_{y}
-summand. Hence (
257
) gives

\displaystyle\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{y}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\omega}^{c}}\right]\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\qquad\leq n^{2}\exp\left\{-\frac{\gamma_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}^{2}}{4}\right\}\leq\frac{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{6}}{n^{2}},

(258)

where the last inequality follows from

\gamma_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}^{2}\geq 4\log\!\bigl(n^{4}/(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{6}\bigr)
.
Combining this bound with (
252
),
(
253
), and (
254
)
proves the lemma.

I.5.9
Proof of Lemma
29

Fix
1\leq\beta\leq 3
. Throughout the proof, write

x=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}y+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\omega,\,y\in{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathsf{data}},\,\omega\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},

By the definition of
\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}
, we can write

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=1}^{n}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\bigr)}{D(x)}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=1}^{n}\left(X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\bigr)}{D(x)}.

(259)

For a truncation radius
\rho_{t}>0
to be chosen later, decompose the right
hand side into a local part and a tail part:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{t}(y,\omega)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{r=1}^{n}\left(X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\bigr)}{D(x)}\mathbbm{1}\{\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}\leq\rho_{t}\},

(260a)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(y,\omega)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{r=1}^{n}\left(X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\frac{\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\bigr)}{D(x)}\mathbbm{1}\{\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\}.

(260b)

Then
\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)={\mathcal{A}}_{t}(y,\omega)+{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(y,\omega)
. On the support of

{\mathcal{A}}_{t}(y,\omega)
, we have

\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}\leq\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}+\|y-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}.

Therefore, after separating the event

\{\|y-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}\leq\rho_{t}\}
from its complement, we obtain the
pointwise bound

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{A}}_{t}(y,\omega)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{t}+R_{0}\mathbbm{1}\{\|y-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\}.

(261)

For the tail part, we use the following term, which has been defined in Lemma
28
,

\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)\coloneqq\frac{\sum_{r=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}\{\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-y\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\bigr)}{D(x)}.

Since
\|X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}\leq R_{0}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(y,\omega)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq R_{0}\,\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega).

(262)

Using
(a+b)^{2\beta}\lesssim a^{2\beta}+b^{2\beta}
, and then applying
(
261
) and
(
262
), we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2\beta}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{\mathcal{A}}_{t}(y,\omega)\|_{2}^{2(\beta-1)}\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2}+\|{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(y,\omega)\|_{2}^{2(\beta-1)}\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{t}^{2(\beta-1)}\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2}+R_{0}^{2(\beta-1)}\mathbbm{1}\{\|y-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\}\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+R_{0}^{2\beta}\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)^{2}.

(263)

The middle term is controlled by the posterior concentration event. Indeed,
since
\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}\leq R_{0}
,

R_{0}^{2(\beta-1)}\mathbbm{1}\{\|y-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\}\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2}\leq R_{0}^{2\beta}\mathbbm{1}\{\|y-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\}.

Next split the first term in (
263
) according
to the event

{\mathcal{E}}_{x}=\left\{\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)\geq\frac{1}{2}p_{X_{t}}(x)\right\}
.
This gives the pointwise inequality

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2\beta}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{t}^{2(\beta-1)}\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}}+\rho_{t}^{2(\beta-1)}\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}^{c}}

\displaystyle\qquad+R_{0}^{2\beta}\mathbbm{1}\{\|y-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\}+R_{0}^{2\beta}\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{t}^{2(\beta-1)}\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}}+\rho_{t}^{2\beta}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}^{c}}

\displaystyle\qquad+R_{0}^{2\beta}\mathbbm{1}\{\|y-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\}+R_{0}^{2\beta}\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)^{2}.

(264)

Taking expectation over
X_{t}
and
X^{\mathsf{sample}}
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\left[\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{2}^{2\beta}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{t}^{2(\beta-1)}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\left[\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{X_{t}}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho_{t}^{2\beta}\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{P}\!\left(\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)<\frac{1}{2}p_{X_{t}}(x)\right)\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\qquad+R_{0}^{2\beta}{\mathbb{P}}\!\left(\|Y-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+R_{0}^{2\beta}\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)^{2}\right]\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y.

(265)

By Lemma
27
,

\displaystyle\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{P}\!\left(\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)<\frac{1}{2}p_{X_{t}}(x)\right)\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}.

(266)

By Lemma
28
, taking
\lambda_{t}=\rho_{t}/2
gives

\displaystyle\int_{y,\omega}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathsf{R}_{t}(y,\omega)^{2}\right]\varphi_{1}(\omega)p_{X_{0}}(y){\textnormal{d}}\omega{\textnormal{d}}y\lesssim\frac{N_{\rho_{t}/2}}{n}+\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{6}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{6}}{n^{2}}\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}}{n\rho_{t}^{k^{\star}}}+\frac{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{6}}{n^{2}}.

(267)

Moreover, Lemma
2
, together with the choice of

\rho_{t}
below, implies

\displaystyle R_{0}^{2\beta}{\mathbb{P}}\!\left(\|Y-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{2}>\rho_{t}\right)\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{2\beta}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2\beta}}{n^{2}}.

(268)

It remains to control the first term on the right-hand side of
(
265
). We first split according to the
typical region:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\left[\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{X_{t}}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\left[\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}}\right]p_{X_{t}}(x){\textnormal{d}}x+R_{0}^{2}{\mathbb{P}}(X_{t}\notin{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}).

(269)

For
x\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}
, on
{\mathcal{E}}_{x}
the denominator satisfies

D(x)=n\widehat{p}_{X_{t}}(x)\geq np_{X_{t}}(x)/2
. Therefore

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\left[\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}}\right]p_{X_{t}}(x){\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\left[\left\|\sum_{r=1}^{n}\left(X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\bigr)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]}{n^{2}p_{X_{t}}(x)}{\textnormal{d}}x.

(270)

For every fixed
x
, the numerator summands are centered:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}}\!\left[\left(X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\bigr)\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\int z\,\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}z-x\bigr)p_{0}(z){\textnormal{d}}z-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\int\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}z-x\bigr)p_{0}(z){\textnormal{d}}z

\displaystyle\quad=\mu_{0\mid t}(x)p_{X_{t}}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)p_{X_{t}}(x)=0.

(271)

Hence all cross terms vanish, and independence yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\left[\left\|\sum_{r=1}^{n}\left(X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right)\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{r}^{\mathsf{sample}}-x\bigr)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=n\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}}\!\left[\left\|X_{0}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x\bigr)\right].

(272)

Combining (
270
) and
(
272
), we get

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\left[\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(x)-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}}\right]p_{X_{t}}(x){\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{1}{n}\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}}\!\left[\left\|X_{0}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x\bigr)\right]}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}{\textnormal{d}}x.

(273)

For the last integral, use

\left\|X_{0}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\left\|X_{0}-y\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|y-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}.

The first part is bounded by Lemma
25
with
\beta=2
,
whereas the second part is bounded by Lemma
26
with

\beta=2
. Thus

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}}\!\left[\left\|X_{0}-\mu_{0\mid t}(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}^{2}\bigl(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x\bigr)\right]}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}{\textnormal{d}}x

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt\lesssim\frac{k^{\star}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{n\,\overline{\alpha}_{t}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2-1}}+\frac{R_{0}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}{n}.

(274)

Together with Lemma
1
, this gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\left[\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{X_{t}}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k^{\star}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{n\,\overline{\alpha}_{t}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2-1}}+\frac{R_{0}^{2}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}{n}.

(275)

Here
\overline{\alpha}_{t}
is bounded below by an absolute constant in the
regime where the DDIM training guarantee is used, so the factor

\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{-1}
is absorbed below.

Substituting (
266
),
(
267
),
(
268
), and
(
275
) into
(
265
), we obtain, for every admissible

\rho_{t}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\left[\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{2}^{2\beta}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{t}^{2(\beta-1)}\frac{k^{\star}R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\rho_{t}^{2\beta}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}+\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+2\beta}}{n\rho_{t}^{k^{\star}}}+\frac{R_{0}^{2\beta}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2\beta}}{n^{2}}.

(276)

For
\beta\leq 3
, the first term in
(
276
) is dominated by the optimized
algebraic terms below after increasing the logarithmic factor. We therefore balance

\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}}\rho_{t}^{2\beta}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}}\asymp\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+2\beta}}{\rho_{t}^{k^{\star}}},

which is equivalent to

\rho_{t}^{k^{\star}+2\beta}\asymp R_{0}^{2\beta}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{k^{\star}/2}
.
Thus we choose

\rho_{t}\asymp R_{0}^{\frac{2\beta}{k^{\star}+2\beta}}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2(k^{\star}+2\beta)}}\sqrt{\log\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}}
.
Substituting this choice into
(
276
) gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X^{\mathsf{sample}}}\left[\|\widehat{\mu}_{0\mid t}(X_{t})-\mu_{0\mid t}(X_{t})\|_{2}^{2\beta}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{R_{0}^{k^{\star}+\frac{4\beta^{2}}{k^{\star}+2\beta}}}{n(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{k^{\star}}{2}-\frac{\beta k^{\star}}{k^{\star}+2\beta}}}\log^{\beta}\frac{dnR_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}+\frac{R_{0}^{2\beta}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2\beta}}{n^{2}}.

(277)

This is the desired bound.
